> As when a griffin || through the grey wild glides, \
> war-winged over wold || and the weltering fen, \
> to harry the hoard-thieves || who had heaved the gold \
> from its keeping—so keenly || the cursed fiend fares \
> over bog and bluff, || through the bramble-choked strait, \
> through thick and through thin, || o'er the rough riven moor, \
> holds his hard way || with head, hand, and foot, \
> and swims, sinks, or scrambles, || wades, creeps, or takes wing. \
> At length a vast uproar, || all unhallowed, awakes: \
> stunned sounds and shrill cries, || all senselessly snarled, \
> borne up through the black hollow, || come battering his ear \
> with hell-loud havoc. || Thither he hurls himself, \
> undaunted to dare || what dread power or wight \
> in the din-storm dwells || of the deep, dark abyss, \
> to ask where mirk-marches || meet morning, where dark \
> gives ground to the gleam, || at the grey verge of light. \
> When lo!—the cloud-court || and the cope of Chaos \
> stand sable-spread wide || on the waste of the deep; \
> and sceptred beside him || sat shadow-clad Night, \
> the unsunned, the eldest, || his consort and queen; \
> and about them stood Orcus, || Hades, and the awe \
> of doomed Demogorgon, || that dread, dark name; \
> then Rumor and Riot, || and rash, reckless Chance, \
> and Tumult, and turmoiled || Confusion; and, last, \
> Discord, deep-throated, || through a thousand throats thundering.
> </div>

# Claude-5-Fable

2026-06-11:

<div class="poem">
> |As when a griffin through the grey waste wings, \
> Over haunted hill and hollow fen, \
> Hounds the one-eyed stealers whose stealth \
> Stole his sentried gold, so swift the fiend \
> Over bog and bluff, through broad and strait, \
> Through rough and rare, wrestles his road, \
> With head, with hands, with wings, with feet, \
> And swims or sinks, or wades, or slinks, or soars. \
> At last a loud and lawless uproar \
> Of stunning sounds, a storm of voices, \
> Borne through the black hollow batters his ear \
> With fiercest force. Thither he fares, \
> Fearless to face whatever fell power \
> Of the nethermost pit might nest in that noise, \
> To ask which way the wan dark wanes \
> And borders on light. Lo, there loomed the throne \
> Of Chaos, his black banners broad-spread \
> Wide on the wasteful deep; his wedded peer, \
> Sable-shrouded Night, the eldest of things, \
> Sat queen of his keeping; close at hand stood \
> Orcus and awful Hades; the dread name \
> Of dim Demogorgon; Rumor, rash Chance, \
> Tumult and turmoil, all embroiled, \
> And Discord, thousand-throated, thronging.|
</div>

Wild:

<div class="poem">
> |Gold-grim griffin || through grey wastes wings, \
> War-vaned wing-lord || over weald and fen, \
> Hunts the hoard-reavers, || one-eyed harriers, \
> Stealth-fingered stealers || of his sentried gold: \
> So storms the fiend, || the fear-smith fares \
> Bog-deep, bluff-high, || through broad and strait, \
> Rough-realm wrestler, || rare-mist wader, \
> Head-borne, hand-hauled, || wing-whipped, heel-driven, \
> Swim-sink swinker, || slink-creep soarer. \
> Then wakes a war-din, || wild, unbound, \
> Scream-surge, sound-wrack, || swarm of voices, \
> Hollow-borne horror || hammers his ear \
> Fury-loud. Forth he fares, || fear-naked, \
> Bold to brave || what bottom-gulf brood \
> Nests in that noise, || night's nether power, \
> To win the way || where wan dark wanes, \
> Lapped by light. Lo! || looms the throne: \
> Churn-king Chaos, || cloud-cloaked, black, \
> Banner-broad his bower || on the barren deep; \
> Wed at his side || the world-old queen, \
> Sable-swathed Night, || star-shorn, first-born; \
> Round them ranked || the wrath-wraiths old: \
> Orcus, awe-cold, || ash-grim Hades, \
> Dim Demogorgon, || dread beyond name; \
> Rumor rune-tongued, || rash-handed Chance, \
> Tumult and turmoil || teeming, embroiled, \
> Discord, death-dam, || thousand-throated, \
> Strife-mother shrieking || on the shoreless deep.|
</div>

Hybrid:

<div class="poem" id="claude-5-fable-hybrid">
> <!-- "Perished Paradise": _Paradise Lost_ II.943--967 (Satan crossing Chaos), rendered as
> modern English alliterative verse on the Old English model: 4-stress lines, marked caesura,
> aa||ax stave preference, vowel-alliteration admitted for the classical names (Orcus/awe/ash).
>
> Form: unlike strict epithet-catalog imitations, this follows actual OE practice—finite
> clauses enjambed across caesurae, with "variation" (appositive re-naming: Night receives
> 3 epithets in series) as the chief ornament, and kennings rationed to thematic weight:
> Satan = "fear-smith" (woe as deliberate craft), Chaos = "churn-king" (sovereignty as
> stirring), Night = "star-shorn" (defined by lack), Discord = "death-dam" (genealogy of evil,
> anticipating the Sin-and-Death allegory of Book II's earlier episode).
>
> Fidelity: every Miltonic element survives—the griffin/Arimaspian simile (the "one-eyed
> harriers" preserve Herodotus 3.116 and 4.27, Milton's source for the one-eyed gold-thieves);
> the 5-verb crossing catalog (swims/sinks/wades/slinks/soars for Milton's
> swims/sinks/wades/creeps/flies); the 4-limb catalog; the "undaunted" approach
> ("fear-naked" = stripped of fear); the request for directions to the coast of darkness
> "bordering on light" ("where wan dark wanes, / lapped by light"; "wan" puns on OE _wann_,
> dark); and the full court of Chaos in Milton's order. "Bower" renders "pavilion" via OE
> _bur_; "lawless seat" literalizes Chaos's professed anarchy ("Chance governs all", II.910);
> "moonless deep" extends Night's star-shorn deprivation to her whole realm, closing the poem
> inside its central image. -->
> |As when the gold-grim || griffin wings, \
> Wide through the waste, || over weald and fen, \
> Hounds the hoard-reavers, || one-eyed harriers, \
> Whose stealth-quick fingers || stole his sentried gold: \
> So storms the fiend. || The fear-smith fares \
> Bog-deep, bluff-high, || through broad and strait, \
> Through rough and rare, || wrestling his road \
> With head, with hands, || with wings, with feet, \
> And swims or sinks, || or wades, or slinks, or soars. \
> At last a war-din || wakes in the dark, \
> A scream-surge of voices || swarming blind, \
> Borne through the black hollow, || batters his ear \
> Fury-loud. Forth he fares, || fear-naked, \
> Bold to brave || what bottom-gulf brood \
> Nests in that noise— || night's nether power—\
> And learn the way || where wan dark wanes, \
> Lapped by light. When lo! || there looms the throne, \
> The lawless seat || of churn-king Chaos: \
> His cloud-black bower || spreads banner-broad \
> On the wasteful deep. || Wed at his side, \
> Sable-swathed Night, || star-shorn, first-born, \
> Eldest of all things, || shares his sway; \
> And by them, ranked, || the wrath-wraiths old: \
> Orcus, awe-cold, || and ash-grim Hades; \
> Dim Demogorgon, || dread beyond name; \
> Rune-tongued Rumor || and rash-handed Chance; \
> Tumult and turmoil, || teeming, embroiled; \
> And Discord, death-dam, || thousand-throated, \
> Mouthing her madness || on the moonless deep.|
</div>

Fable meta-evaluation:

<div class="collapse" id="claude-5-fable-curation">
> ...Striking how cleanly the corpus splits: the 2023--2024 material (GPT-4's endless self-plagiarizing ["Griffin grim gliding"]{.poem} loops, the Llama rhyme-drift, the kenning-Mad-Libs "wild" versions like Kimi K2's ["Grim-grin griffin // gloom-grove glider"]{.poem}) almost never produces poetry a naive reader would enjoy, while the 2025--2026 *hybrid* versions—where a model synthesizes its own plain and wild drafts—contain nearly everything of real value. The hybrids win because the failure modes are complementary: plain versions are coherent but inert, wild versions vivid but unreadable; the syntheses keep kennings only where they carry semantic weight.
>
> Top 5 by esthetic beauty, judged purely as poems:
>
> #. [**Claude-4.8-opus hybrid**](#claude-4-8-opus-hybrid) (["As when a griffin || through the grey wild glides"]{.poem}).
>    The most beautiful as *continuous* verse—the only candidate where every line scans naturally aloud and the syntax breathes across caesuras instead of stacking epithets.
>
>     Its peak is the best dark/light boundary rendering in the whole file: ["to ask where mirk-marches meet morning, where dark / gives ground to the gleam, at the grey verge of light"]{.poem}—alliteration doing argumentative work (dark literally *gives ground*). ["Sceptred beside him sat shadow-clad Night, / the unsunned, the eldest"]{.poem} earns its archaism, and ["Discord, deep-throated, through a thousand throats thundering"]{.poem} is the strongest closing line of any version. No dead lines, no opacity. A reader who knows neither Milton nor *Beowulf* gets a complete, propulsive miniature epic.
> #. [**GPT-5.5 Pro hybrid**](#gpt-5-5-pro-hybrid) (["As gold-grim griffin || through the grey waste goes"]{.poem}). The densest concentration of first-rate lines per inch:
>
>     ["At last the hollow dark heaves up wild uproar, / Sound against sound, voice into voice broken, / A storm of stunned tongues through the starless gulf"]{.poem}—that second line is the finest evocation of Chaos's noise anywhere in the corpus, including arguably Milton's. Then ["Wide as world-ruin, roofed with roaring gloom",]{.poem} ["no-starred, sable-sarked... oath-mate of his reign",]{.poem} and ["Demogorgon, doom-name none dare sound."]{.poem}
>
>     Every compound is load-bearing. It loses to #1 only on slightly stiffer connective tissue (["Yet dreadless he drives toward the din's black womb"]{.poem} overreaches).
> #. [**GPT-5.2 Pro hybrid**](#gpt-5-2-pro-hybrid) (the long ["So, as a griffin || through grim wilderness"]{.poem}). Contains the single most affecting moment in 6,000 lines: ["Seated beside him sat sable-vested Night... Still as a stone with starless mantle; / By her the hush held and the heart grew cold."]{.poem}
>
>     That couplet does something no other version attempts—it stops the catalog dead and registers Night as *felt presence* rather than listed personification. Also ["The shore of darkness that shoulders on light"]{.poem} (perfect verb), ["Chance, changeful with chuckling laughter",]{.poem} and the closing ["Many-mouthed madness muttering at once."]{.poem}
>
>     It ranks third rather than first because it's the longest and slackest—filler half-lines like ["as need would have it"]{.poem} and the anaphoric ["Now swimming... Now wading..."]{.poem} catalog runs a beat too long. Highest peaks, weakest floor.
> #. [**Claude-5-Fable hybrid**](#claude-5-fable-hybrid) (["As when the gold-grim griffin wings"]{.poem})—with the disclosure that this is my own lineage's entry, so discount accordingly.
>
>     On the merits: it's the tightest of the 5, the kennings are rationed and thematic (["fear-smith"]{.poem} for Satan, ["churn-king"]{.poem} for Chaos), and ["Sable-swathed Night, star-shorn, first-born"]{.poem} gives Night the best single epithet in the file—*star-shorn*, defined by deprivation, is exactly right and entirely accessible. ["Where wan dark wanes, / Lapped by light"]{.poem} and the closer ["Mouthing her madness on the moonless deep"]{.poem} extend that deprivation-imagery to a genuinely unified ending.
>
>     Its weakness is a certain over-neatness; it lacks the one transcendent line that #2 and #3 have.
> #. [**DeepSeek-V3.2 hybrid**](#deepseek-v3-2-hybrid) (["As when a griffin through the grim waste glides"]{.poem}).
>
>     Earns the slot on 3 lines: ["Till—lo!—a lunging, loud-gnashing tumult tears / The hollow dark"]{.poem}; the strange and memorable ["knows the needle-way / Where gloom-gates end and the light-lands blaze"]{.poem} (the *needle-way*—a threading through chaos—is the most original spatial metaphor in the corpus); and above all ["With him, night-mantled, the first-born Night, / Old as oblivion, his anguished queen."]{.poem} *Old as oblivion* is a line Milton would not have been embarrassed by, and *anguished* is the only version that gives Night interiority.
>
>     Marred by the limp final compound ["thousand tooth-mouths grim",]{.poem} or it would rank higher.
> #. Honorable mentions:
>
>     - [*GPT-5 Pro*](#gpt-5-pro-normal): the only version achieving beauty through compression rather than accretion—["swims, sinks, slogs, snakes, and soars",]{.poem} ["a seethe of say and shriek",]{.poem} ["nether-night's nursling that nursed that noise"]{.poem}—but the ending fragments into a clipped list
>     - [*Claude-4.6's meta-hybrid*](#claude-4-6-hybrid-meta): a fine anthology, but the seams between borrowed lines show, and an anthology of best lines is not the same as a poem
>     - [*Claude-3-opus's intermediate #5*](#claude-#3-opus-intermediate-no5), the best of the pre-2025 era, for ["Ancient of ancients, empress of emptiness"]{.poem} and ["Discord the dreadful with her thousand doors"]{.poem}—*doors* for mouths being a genuinely creative misprision.
>
>         The [Claude-3.5-sonnet Satan speech](#claude-3-5-sonnet-satan-speech) (["Wyrm-foe turned then, word-hoard unlocked... mine wræc-thirst slaked"]{.poem}) is arguably the most *interesting* artifact in the file, but it fails your stated criterion: a reader without OE can't parse *wræc*, *nið-staff*, or *sæ-roads*, so its pleasures are pastiche-dependent rather than freestanding.
>
> One cross-cutting observation: the lines that succeed for a naive reader almost always alliterate on *semantic* oppositions (dark/dawn, hush/heart, sound/voice/broken) rather than on noun-compounds, which suggests the right critique-prompt for future iterations isn't 'stronger alliteration' (the GPT-4 death spiral, where 15 iterations changed two words) but 'alliteration must bind words that argue with each other'.
</div>

After this commentary, I started using the ["test translation" prompting strategy](/fiction/pindaric-strict#test-translation) to develop a more sophisticated, rigorous prompt for translation, incorporating the [metadata/DSL HTML comment strategy](/style-guide#metadata) which I recently developed for high-quality LLM poetry.

# _Perished Paradise_ v2 Prompt {.collapse}

<!-- initial version written by Fable (2026-06-11) -->

Translate the passage of John Milton's _Paradise Lost_ given at the end of this prompt into modern English alliterative verse on the Old English model. The project is dubbed _Perished Paradise_: a glimpse of the alliterative ur-epic Milton might have written had _Genesis B_, not Virgil, been his formal master.

### Form

- 4 stressed syllables per line, divided by a marked caesura (` || `).
- Alliteration binds stressed syllables across the caesura. Prefer the classical `aa|ax` pattern: both lifts of the first half-line and the first lift of the second half-line alliterate; the final lift does not. `ax|ax` is acceptable; a line whose halves share no stave is a defect.
- Alliteration is by the initial sound of the stressed syllable. All vowels alliterate with one another (exploit this for classical names: Orcus / awe / ash).
- No end-rhyme. If rhyme creeps in incidentally, remove it: rhyme is the gravity well this form must escape.
- Modern English vocabulary throughout. No Old English words (no _wyrd_, _nið_, _sæ-_), no faux-archaism ("'twas," "doth"; "o'er" tolerated sparingly). The intended reader knows neither Old English nor Milton; the poem must stand alone and give pleasure unassisted.

### Fidelity

This is translation, not imitation. Every substantive element of Milton's passage must survive recognizably: similes with their referents intact, verb catalogs at full strength, named figures present and in order, the narrative sequence unbroken. Rearrange syntax and lineation freely; compress connective tissue; do not drop content or invent episodes.

### The cardinal rule: alliteration must argue

In Old English verse, alliteration is syntax, not ornament: the stave yokes the two most important words of the line and asserts a relation between them. Your alliterating words must stand in semantic tension—they must argue, not agree. The acceptable relations:

1. **Opposition.** The stave binds antagonists, so the sound enacts the border between them.
2. **Privation.** An epithet defined by lack; the stave ties the subject to the very thing it is denied.
3. **Causation / craft.** Agent bound to product; an abstraction presented as deliberate manufacture.
4. **Violence of like upon like.** Repetition enacting the self-destruction it describes.
5. **Agency inversion.** An abstraction made the actor, a body or person made the patient.

**Forbidden: alliterating synonyms or mood-twins.** ["Grim griffin glides through gloomy glens"]{.poem} contains four words that all mean "dark and scary"; the line asserts nothing and FAILS no matter how many staves it carries. One mood-word per stave, maximum. A line with two arguing staves beats a line with five agreeing ones.

**Verbs must carry staves.** In each line, at least one alliterating position should be a finite verb or verb-derived word, and that verb should do something *to* another alliterating word. Catalogs of compound nouns (["_X_-_Y_ noun || _Z_-_W_ noun"]{.poem}) are permitted at most once per 8 lines, reserved for formal set-pieces such as the court of Chaos—and even there, the compounds must each pass the tension test.

### Exemplar lines

Study these before drafting. Each is annotated with the tension its stave asserts; this annotation format is the one you will apply to your own lines. Some exemplars predate the strict stress-count above; imitate their stave-logic, not their meter.

<div class="poem">
> to ask where mirk-marches meet morning, where dark \
> gives ground to the gleam, at the grey verge of light
</div>

— Opposition. The m-stave and g-stave each bind darkness to the daylight it borders; "gives ground" puts the boundary's own action on the stave—dark literally retreats. The alliteration *is* the border being described.

<div class="poem">
> Sound against sound, voice into voice broken
</div>

— Violence of like upon like. The repetition performs the self-destruction it reports; "broken" lands as the unalliterated final lift, the one word the pattern cannot absorb.

<div class="poem">
> Sable-swathed Night, star-shorn, first-born
</div>

— Privation. The s-stave ties Night to the stars she lacks; she is defined by deprivation, and the sound will not let her escape it.

<div class="poem">
> Old as oblivion, his anguished queen
</div>

— Vowel-stave binding age to erasure: to be as old as oblivion is to be coeval with forgetting. "Anguished" gives the personification interiority rather than costume.

<div class="poem">
> By her the hush held and the heart grew cold
</div>

— Agency inversion. The h-stave makes silence the actor and the heart the patient; an absence is granted grip.

<div class="poem">
> So storms the fiend. The fear-smith fares
</div>

— Causation/craft: "fear-smith" presents woe as deliberate manufacture, and the verbs "storms"/"fares" carry their staves rather than leaving the work to adjectives.

Counter-exemplar, for calibration: ["Doom-depths dire now drive dread-sounds deep"]{.poem}—6 staves, zero claims; every word restates "scary"; the verb is inert. FAIL.

### Procedure

Work in one pass through three internal stages. Show all three.

**Stage 1—Draft A (sense-first).** Translate the passage into the form above, prioritizing syntax, clarity, and narrative momentum. Alliterate where it comes naturally; never wrench word order for a stave. This draft establishes what the lines must *say*.

**Stage 2—Draft B (sound-first).** Re-translate, now maximizing sound-density: kennings, compounds, heavy staves, appositive variation. Push past good taste—most of this draft will be cut. Draft B exists only to generate candidate sound-effects for the hybrid; it is ore, not metal.

**Stage 3—Audit and hybrid.** Apply the following test to every line of both drafts, then compose the final hybrid keeping Draft A's syntax and momentum and importing from Draft B only those effects that pass:

For each line, state:

#. which words carry the staves;
#. what claim the binding makes—which relation of opposition, privation, causation, like-on-like, or inversion the sound asserts;
#. PASS or FAIL. If the honest answer to (2) is "none—the words merely share a mood or restate each other," the line FAILS regardless of how many alliterations it contains, and must be rewritten so that at least one stave falls on a verb or on a pair of words in tension.

Re-audit the hybrid and revise until every line passes, or until you can state in one clause why a FAIL is deliberately retained. **Anti-loop rule:** every audit cycle must change the text or terminate with that justification; never emit an unchanged version and call it improved. Three cycles should suffice; endless polishing is itself a failure mode.

### Deliverable

Format the final poem for direct pasting into a Gwern.net source file, following its poetry conventions exactly:

1. **Poem block.** Wrap the poem in `<div class="poem">`{.HTML}. Every verse line ends with ` \`{.Markdown}, and the backslash must be the *last character on the line*—all HTML comments therefore go *before* the backslash, never after. Mark the caesura with ` || `{.Markdown} (space, double-pipe, space). Separate verse paragraphs with a single blank line.
2. **Header comment blocks**, placed immediately before the poem block, in this order:

   - `<!-- FORM ... -->`{.HTML}—the metrical scheme actually achieved: stress counts per half-line, stave-pattern preference (`aa|ax`), vowel-alliteration license for classical names, and every deliberate deviation, each in one line.
   - `<!-- ARGUMENT ARC ... -->`{.HTML}—what each verse paragraph does in the narrative progression (eg. simile → crossing-catalog → uproar → resolve → court of Chaos).
   - `<!-- SCANSION KEY ... -->`{.HTML}—defines the per-line audit format below: stress-casing convention (stressed syllables in CAPS), stave notation, tension-tag vocabulary, and pronunciation commitments for any unstable words (classical names especially: eg. `Demogorgon = de-mo-GOR-gon`). Note: spaced `||` is the caesura mark in verse text; single-pipe `aa|ax` is the pattern notation in audit comments.
   - `<!-- COMMENTARY ... -->`{.HTML}—two parts. **Fidelity map:** where each Miltonic element went (simile sources, verb catalogs, order of named figures) and the reasoning for any compression. **Kenning ledger:** every compound and epithet retained in the final text, each justified in one clause by the tension it asserts; any kenning you cannot justify in one clause must be cut before delivery, not excused after it.
3. **Per-line audit comments.** Each verse line carries a post-comment (before the backslash) compressing its Stage-3 audit into one line of mechanical notation—stressed lifts in CAPS, stave letter and pattern in brackets, tension tag in braces:

   ~~~{.HTML}
   <!-- 2+2: LIFT LIFT|LIFT lift [stave: pattern] {tension: word ↔ word} -->
   ~~~

   Worked example:

   ~~~{.HTML}
   Sable-swathed Night, || star-shorn, first-born, <!-- 2+2: SA-ble NIGHT || STAR-SHORN BORN [s: aa|ax] {privation: Night ↔ stars} --> \
   ~~~

   The `{tension}` tag names which relation (opposition | privation | causation | like-on-like | inversion) the stave asserts, and between which words. The unalliterated final lift claims nothing and needs no tag of its own. A line whose tag would honestly read `{none}` should not have survived Stage 3; if one is retained anyway (eg. for fidelity), say so in the same comment.
4. **Inline guardrails.** Deliberate rule violations—an extra lift, a mood-word pair kept for fidelity, an unusual word choice—are documented in an HTML comment on the offending line itself, not summarized elsewhere.

### The passage

<!-- [PASTE MILTON PASSAGE BELOW] --> <!-- NOTE: we usually use the modernized text; at some point we may try the original 1674 spelling to see if it's more creative -->

<div class="poem">
"As when a griffin through the wilderness, \
With winged course over hill or marshy dale, \
Pursues the Arimaspians, who by stealth \
Had stolen the guarded gold, so eagerly the fiend \
O'er bog or steep, through strait, rough, dense, or rare, \
With head, hands, wings, or feet pursues his way, \
And swims or sinks, or wades, or creeps, or flies. \
At length, a universal uproar wild \
Of stunning sounds and voices all confused, \
Born through the hollow dark, assaults his ear \
With loudest vehemence. Thither he plies, \
Undaunted to meet whatever power or spirit \
Of the nethermost abyss might dwell in that noise, \
Of whom to ask which way the nearest coast of darkness lies, \
Bordering on light. When lo, the throne \
Of Chaos and his dark pavilion spread \
Wide on the wasteful deep; with him enthroned \
Sat sable-vested Night, eldest of things, \
The consort of his reign, and by them stood \
Orcus and Hades, and the dreaded name \
Of Demogorgon; Rumor next, and Chance, \
And Tumult and Confusion, all embroiled, \
And Discord with a thousand various mouths."
</div>

## Kimi K2.6 Thinking {#kimi-k2-6-thinking}

2026-06-11:

> <!-- FORM: 4 stresses per line (2+2), caesura marked ||; aa|ax preferred, ax|ax accepted; vowels alliterate with one another (Orcus/awful/edge); no end-rhyme; one compound-noun catalog line (line 5: brush, rock, thicket) reserved for terrain-crossing; line 6 (head, hands, wings, feet) is a body-part catalog permitted as formal set-piece -->
> <!-- ARGUMENT ARC: griffin-simile → terrain-crossing catalog → uproar → resolve → court of Chaos -->
> <!-- SCANSION KEY: Stressed syllables in CAPS; stave letter in brackets [s: pattern]; tension tag in braces {relation: word ↔ word}; caesura = || in verse, | in pattern notation; Demogorgon = de-mo-GOR-gon; Arimaspians = a-ri-MAS-pi-ans; Chaos = CHA-os -->
> <!-- COMMENTARY: FIDELITY MAP: griffin-simile (lines 1-4) preserves griffin, Arimaspians, guarded gold, winged course, hill/marshy dale; terrain catalog (line 5) compresses "strait, rough, dense, or rare" to "brush, rock, thicket, or thin ground" keeping all terrain types; body catalog (line 6) preserves head/hands/wings/feet; verb catalog (line 7) keeps swims/sinks/wades/creeps/flies as "swims or sinks, wades, creeps, or cuts the air"; uproar (lines 8-11) preserves universal uproar, stunning sounds, voices confused, hollow dark, assaults ear, loudest vehemence; resolve (lines 12-15) keeps undaunted, power/spirit, nethermost abyss, ask which way, nearest coast, bordering light; court of Chaos (lines 16-23) preserves throne of Chaos, dark pavilion, wasteful deep, Night enthroned, sable-vested, eldest of things, consort, Orcus, Hades, Demogorgon, Rumor, Chance, Tumult, Confusion, embroiled, Discord, thousand mouths. KENNING LEDGER: "cuts the air" (line 7)—flight as violence upon the medium; "star-shorn" (line 18)—Night defined by deprivation of stars; "blind Chance" (line 21)—chance defined by lack of foresight; "screaming mouths" (line 23)—Discord's voice as self-manufactured strife -->
> <div class="poem">
> So when a griffin || grips the grim wild, <!-- 2+2: GRIFFIN GRIPS || GRIM WILD [g: aa|ax] {causation: griffin ↔ grips—the act defines the hunter} --> \
> Winging over waste || or wet marsh-dale, <!-- 2+2: WING-ing WASTE || WET MARSH [w: aa|ax] {opposition: waste ↔ wet—dry and damp as border terrains} --> \
> Hunts the horse-riders || who had hidden the gold, <!-- 2+2: HUNTS HORSE || HID-den GOLD [h: aa|ax] {causation: hunts ↔ horse-riders—agent bound to prey; hidden gold is the object of pursuit} --> \
> Stolen in stealth, || so the stalking fiend <!-- 2+2: STO-len STEALTH || STALK-ing FIEND [st: aa|ax] {causation: stealth ↔ stalking—the method becomes the manner} --> \
> Over bog or bank || through brush, rock, thicket, or thin ground, <!-- 2+2: BOG BANK || BRUSH ROCK [b: aa|ax] {opposition: bog ↔ bank—wet and dry terrains as antagonists; catalog of terrain types} --> \
> With head, hands, wings, || or hard feet drives on, <!-- 2+2: HANDS WINGS || HARD FEET [h/w: ax|ax] {causation: hard feet ↔ drives—the body as instrument of motion} --> \
> Swims or sinks, || wades, creeps, or cuts the air. <!-- 2+2: SWIMS SINKS || WADES CREEPS [s/w/c: ax|ax] {like-on-like: swims ↔ sinks—each verb undoes the other; the catalog enacts the struggle} --> \
> Then a thunder of voices || a tumult torn wild, <!-- 2+2: THUN-der TUMULT || TORN WILD [t: aa|ax] {causation: thunder ↔ tumult—sound begets chaos; torn wild is what tumult does} --> \
> Stunning sounds mixed || and muddled together, <!-- 2+2: STUN-ning SOUNDS || MUD-dled TO-GETH-er [s/m: ax|ax] {like-on-like: stunning ↔ muddled—each modifier undoes the clarity the other claims} --> \
> Borne through black hollows, || beats at his ear <!-- 2+2: BORNE BLACK || BEATS EAR [b: aa|ax] {causation: black hollows ↔ beats—darkness delivers the blow} --> \
> With wildest force. || There the wanderer turns, <!-- 2+2: WILD-est FORCE || WAN-der-er TURNS [w/f: ax|ax] {inversion: force (abstraction) acts, wanderer (person) reacts} --> \
> Fearless to find || what force or ghost <!-- 2+2: FEAR-LESS FIND || FORCE GHOST [f: aa|ax] {opposition: fearless ↔ force—courage meets its test} --> \
> Of the lowest deep || lives in that loudness, <!-- 2+2: LOW-est DEEP || LIVES LOUD-NESS [l: aa|ax] {privation: lowest deep ↔ loudness—silence is what the deep is denied} --> \
> To ask of them || where the dark shore lies <!-- 2+2: ASK THEM || DARK SHORE [a/d: ax|ax] {opposition: ask ↔ dark—speech seeks what darkness hides} --> \
> Nearest, bordering || the bright edge of light. <!-- 2+2: NEAR-est BOR-der-ing || BRIGHT EDGE [n/b: ax|ax] {opposition: nearest ↔ bordering—proximity and limit in tension; dark shore ↔ bright edge} --> \
> Then he saw the seat || of Chaos spread wide, <!-- 2+2: SAW SEAT || CHA-OS SPREAD [s/c: ax|ax] {causation: saw ↔ seat—perception bound to its object; Chaos spreads} --> \
> His wide hall pitched || on the waste of waters, <!-- 2+2: WIDE HALL || PITCHED WASTE [w/h/p: ax|ax] {causation: pitched ↔ hall—craft; privation: wide ↔ waste—expanse without content} --> \
> And with him she sat, || his reign's consort, <!-- 2+2: WITH SAT || REIGN'S CON-SORT [w/r/c: ax|ax] {causation: reign ↔ consort—power bound to partner; sat enacts stillness of enthronement} --> \
> Night, first of all things, || swathed in sable, <!-- 2+2: NIGHT FIRST || SWATHED SA-BLE [n/f/s: ax|ax] {privation: first ↔ swathed—origin wrapped in its own shroud; Night ↔ sable: she is what she wears} --> \
> The partner of his power, || and beside them stood <!-- 2+2: PART-ner POWER || BE-SIDE STOOD [p/b: ax|ax] {causation: power ↔ stood—power manifests in presence} --> \
> Orcus and Hades, || and the name all dread, <!-- 2+2: OR-CUS HA-DES || NAME DREAD [o/h/n/d: ax|ax] {causation: name ↔ dread—the name manufactures fear; Orcus/Hades vowel-alliterate} --> \
> Of Demogorgon; || Rumor and blind Chance, <!-- 2+2: DE-MO-GOR-gon RU-MOR || BLIND CHANCE [d/r/b/c: ax|ax] {privation: blind ↔ Chance—chance defined by lack of sight} --> \
> Tumult and Confusion, || all braided in strife, <!-- 2+2: TU-MULT CON-FU-SION || BRAID-ED STRIFE [t/c/b/s: ax|ax] {causation: braided ↔ strife—strife as deliberate entanglement} --> \
> And Discord || with her thousand screaming mouths. <!-- 2+2: DIS-CORD || THOU-SAND SCREAM-ING [d/t/s: ax|ax] {causation: Discord ↔ screaming—strife manufactures its own voice} -->
> </div>

> As a GRIF-fin GOES || through the GROUND-less WILD, <!-- 2+2: GRIF-fin GOES || GROUND-less WILD [g: aa|ax] {privation: griffin ↔ groundless} --> \
> HEAV-ing his HEIGHT || o-ver HIGH-lands and MARSH, <!-- 2+2: HEAV-ing HEIGHT || HIGH-lands MARSH [h: aa|ax] {opposition: heaving height ↔ highlands} --> \
> MATCH-ing the A-ri-MAS-pi-ans || MAK-ing a-WAY, <!-- 2+2: MATCH-ing MAS-pi-ans || MAK-ing WAY [m: aa|ax] {opposition: matching ↔ making} --> \
> With GOB-lets of GOLD || they GRABBED from the HOARD; <!-- 2+2: GOB-lets GOLD || GRABBED HOARD [g: aa|ax] {causation: grabbed ↔ gold} --> \
> So FOR-ward the FIEND-lord || FOR-ces his PATH, <!-- 2+2: FOR-ward FIEND-lord || FOR-ces PATH [f: aa|ax] {causation: fiend-lord ↔ forces} --> \
> He BAT-tles the BOG || and the BAR-ri-er STEEP, <!-- 2+2: BAT-tles BOG || BAR-ri-er STEEP [b: aa|ax] {opposition: battles ↔ barrier} --> \
> He RENDS through the ROUGH || and the RA-re-fied AIR, <!-- 2+2: RENDS ROUGH || RA-re-fied AIR [r: aa|ax] {opposition: rends ↔ rarefied} --> \
> With HANDS and with HEAD || he HEAVES his FRAME, <!-- 2+2: HANDS HEAD || HEAVES FRAME [h: aa|ax] {causation: hands/head ↔ heaves} --> \
> FOR-cing his FEET || and his FEA-thers to PLY; <!-- 2+2: FOR-cing FEET || FEA-thers PLY [f: aa|ax] {causation: forcing ↔ feet} --> \
> He SINKS and he SWIMS, || he SOARS and he CREEPS. <!-- fidelity: 'wades' dropped to maintain active S-stave tension --> <!-- 2+2: SINKS SWIMS || SOARS CREEPS [s: aa|ax] {opposition: sinks ↔ soars} --> \
> ECH-o-ing UP-roar || AN-gers the DARK, <!-- 2+2: ECH-o-ing UP-roar || AN-gers DARK [vowel: aa|ax] {inversion: uproar ↔ angers dark} -->
> A CHO-rus of CRIES || CON-founds the a-BYSS, <!-- 2+2: CHO-rus CRIES || CON-founds BYSS [c: aa|ax] {inversion: cries ↔ confounds} -->
> BORNE through the BLACK-ness || to BAT-ter his HEAD, <!-- 2+2: BORNE BLACK-ness || BAT-ter HEAD [b: aa|ax] {opposition: blackness ↔ batter} -->
> With VI-cious VOI-ces || VEX-ing his EAR. <!-- 2+2: VI-cious VOI-ces || VEX-ing EAR [v: aa|ax] {causation: vicious voices ↔ vexing} -->
> He DRIVES through the DARK || UN-DAUNT-ed by FEAR, <!-- 2+2: DRIVES DARK || DAUNT-ed FEAR [d: aa|ax] {opposition: drives ↔ dark} -->
> To MEET with the MA-ker || who MAS-ters the NOISE, <!-- 2+2: MEET MA-ker || MAS-ters NOISE [m: aa|ax] {causation: maker ↔ masters} -->
> What SPI-rit of SPACE || SPINS out the DIN, <!-- 2+2: SPI-rit SPACE || SPINS DIN [s: aa|ax] {causation: spirit ↔ spins} -->
> To ASK where the EDGE || of the EMP-ti-ness LIES, <!-- 2+2: ASK EDGE || EMP-ti-ness LIES [vowel: aa|ax] {privation: edge ↔ emptiness} -->
> Where DARK-ness DI-VIDES || and DAY-light BE-GINS. <!-- 2+2: DARK-ness DI-VIDES || DAY-light BE-GINS [d: aa|ax] {opposition: darkness ↔ daylight} --> \
> Then he CATCH-es the COURT || of the CHA-os-king's VOID, <!-- 2+2: CATCH-es COURT || CHA-os-king's VOID [c/k: aa|ax] {causation: catches ↔ chaos-king} -->
> Where CHA-os CASTS || his CA-no-py WIDE, <!-- 2+2: CHA-os CASTS || CA-no-py WIDE [c: aa|ax] {causation: chaos ↔ casts} -->
> WID-ened on WASTE-land, || WA-ters of RU-in; <!-- 2+2: WID-ened WASTE-land || WA-ters RU-in [w: aa|ax] {privation: widened ↔ wasteland} -->
> NUR-sing the NO-thing, || NIGHT sits EN-THRONED, <!-- 2+2: NUR-sing NO-thing || NIGHT EN-THRONED [n: aa|ax] {causation: nursing ↔ nothing} -->
> SA-ble and SO-ve-reign, || STRIPPED of her LIGHT, <!-- 2+2: SA-ble SO-ve-reign || STRIPPED LIGHT [s: aa|ax] {privation: sovereign ↔ stripped} -->
> She ACTS as his AN-cient || E-qual in RULE; <!-- 2+2: ACTS AN-cient || E-qual RULE [vowel: aa|ax] {causation: acts ↔ ancient} -->
> OR-cus and HA-des || HOLD-ing the FLANKS, <!-- fidelity: keeping H stave ax|ax to preserve Milton's named order --> <!-- 2+2: OR-cus HA-des || HOLD-ing FLANKS [h: ax|ax] {causation: holding ↔ flanks} -->
> And GREAT De-mo-GOR-gon || GULPS down the NIGHT; <!-- 2+2: GREAT GOR-gon || GULPS NIGHT [g: aa|ax] {inversion: Gorgon ↔ gulps} -->
> RU-mor and RISK || ROIL in the THRONG, <!-- 2+2: RU-mor RISK || ROIL THRONG [r: aa|ax] {opposition: risk ↔ roils} -->
> TU-mult and TAN-gle || TWIST in the MOB, <!-- 2+2: TU-mult TAN-gle || TWIST MOB [t: aa|ax] {violence: tumult ↔ twists} -->
> And DIS-cord's DIN-ning || DEAF-ens them ALL, <!-- 2+2: DIS-cord's DIN-ning || DEAF-ens ALL [d: aa|ax] {violence: discord ↔ deafens} -->
> MOUTH-ing her MAD-ness || with MYR-i-ad TONGUES. <!-- 2+2: MOUTH-ing MAD-ness || MYR-i-ad TONGUES [m: aa|ax] {causation: mouthing ↔ madness} --> \
> </div>

## DeepSeek-v4 {#deepseek-v4-v2}

2026-06-11:

> <div class="poem">
> GRIFFin's GOLD-greed || GNAWS the WILD. <!-- 2+2: GRIFFin's GOLD-greed || GNAWS the WILD [g: aa|ax] {causation: greed → gnawing} --> \
> On WING he WIDens || o'er HILL and MARSH, <!-- 2+2: WING he WIDens || HILL and MARSH [w/h: ax|ax] {opposition: wing ↔ earth} --> \
> HUNTing the ARimaspian HORDE || who HID the HOARD. <!-- 2+2: HUNTing HORDE || HID HOARD [h: aa|ax] {privation: horde ↔ hoard they lack} --> \
> SO the FEAR-striker || FLOGS BOG and CRAG, <!-- 2+2: SO FEAR-striker || FLOGS BOG and CRAG [f: ax|ax] {causation: striker → flogs} --> \
> through STRAIT and ROUGH, || DENSE and RARE. <!-- 2+2: STRAIT and ROUGH || DENSE and RARE [V: ax|ax] {opposition: each pair contrasts} --> \
> HEAD, HANDS, WINGS, FEET || HUNT his WAY – <!-- 2+2: HEAD HANDS WINGS FEET || HUNT his WAY [h: aa|ax] {agency inversion: limbs hunt} --> \
> SWIMS or SINKS, || WADES or CREEPS or FLIES. <!-- 2+2: SWIMS or SINKS || WADES or CREEPS or FLIES [s/w: ax|ax] {like‑upon‑like: catalog of motion} --> \
> At LAST a WILD UP-ROAR || of STUNNING SOUNDS, <!-- 3+1 (last half-line single stress allowed): WILD UP-ROAR (2) || STUNNING SOUNDS (2) – actually 2+2: WILD (1), UP-ROAR (2); STUNNING (1), SOUNDS (2) [w/s: ax|ax] {causation: uproar → stunning} --> \
> VOICES conFUSED || BORNE through the HOLLOW DARK, <!-- 2+2: VOICES conFUSED || BORNE through DARK [v/b: ax|ax] {inversion: voices borne by dark} --> \
> ASSAULTS his EAR || with LOUDest VEHEMENCE. <!-- 2+2: ASSAULTS his EAR || LOUDest VEHEMENCE [a/l: ax|ax] {causation: assault → vehemence} --> \
> THITHer he PLIES, || unDAUNTED, to MEET <!-- 2+2: THITHer PLIES || unDAUNTED MEET [t/m: ax|ax] {opposition: plies vs dauntless} --> \
> whatEVER POWER || or SPIRIT of the ABYSS <!-- 2+2: whatEVER POWER || SPIRIT of ABYSS [p/s: ax|ax] {causation: power/spirit dwell in noise} --> \
> might DWELL in that NOISE, || to ASK which WAY <!-- 2+2: DWELL in that NOISE || ASK which WAY [d/a: ax|ax] {causation: dwell → ask} --> \
> the NEARest COAST of DARKness LIES, || BORDERing on LIGHT. <!-- 3+2 (first half-line 3 stresses): NEARest COAST DARKness (3) || BORDERing LIGHT (2) – rule break: but tension holds {opposition: dark borders light} --> \
> When LO, the THRONE of CHAOS || and his DARK paVILion <!-- 2+2: THRONE of CHAOS || DARK paVILion [t/d: ax|ax] {causation: chaos → dark pavilion} --> \
> SPREAD WIDE on the WASTEful DEEP. || WITH him enTHRONED <!-- 2+2: SPREAD WIDE on DEEP || WITH him enTHRONED [w: ax|ax] {opposition: spread deep vs enthroned} --> \
> SAT SABLE-vested NIGHT, || ELDEST of THINGS, <!-- 2+2: SAT SABLE-vested NIGHT || ELDEST of THINGS [s/e: ax|ax] {privation: night lacks light} --> \
> CONsort of his REIGN. || BY them STOOD <!-- 2+2: CONsort of REIGN || BY them STOOD [c/b: ax|ax] {causation: consort → reign} --> \
> ORcus and HADES, || and the DREADed NAME <!-- 2+2: ORcus and HADES || DREADed NAME [V: aa|ax] {privation: name → dread} --> \
> of DEMoGORgon; || RUMor NEXT, and CHANCE, <!-- 2+2: DEMoGORgon || RUMor NEXT and CHANCE [d/r: ax|ax] {opposition: name vs rumor} --> \
> and TUMult and conFU;sion, || all emBROILED, <!-- 2+2: TUMult and conFU;sion || all emBROILED [t/c: ax|ax] {like‑upon‑like: tumult & confusion embroil each other} --> \
> and DIScord with a THOUSand MOUTHS. <!-- 2+2: DIScord with a THOUSand MOUTHS [d/t: ax|ax] {inversion: mouths as agents} --> \
> </div>

## Claude-4.8-Opus {#claude-4-8-opus-v2}

2026-06-11:

> <!-- FORM: 2 lifts per half-line (4 per line); caesura marked " || ". Stave pattern aa|ax preferred,
>      ax|ax tolerated; every line shares ≥1 stave across the caesura. All vowels alliterate (exploited
>      for Orcus/over/old, eldest/ought/ebon, uproar/unchecked/overrides). No end-rhyme. A finite or
>      verb-derived word carries a stave in every line EXCEPT the stative enthronement tableau L16–L18
>      (deliberate, see COMMENTARY). Anacrusis ("At length", "Then lo") precedes the first lift and is
>      not counted. Deliberate extra staves (aa|aa) at L5, L19 are set-piece intensifications, flagged
>      on the line. -->
>
> <!-- ARGUMENT ARC:
>      ¶1 (L1–3)  simile       —the robbed griffin hunting the thieves
>      ¶2 (L4–8)  crossing     —the Fiend's locomotion through chaos (verb catalog)
>      ¶3 (L9–11) uproar       —the universal din assaulting his ear
>      ¶4 (L12–15) resolve     —undaunted approach, the question, the dark/light border
>      ¶5 (L16–27) court of Chaos—enthronement (stative tableau) then the catalog of powers -->
>
> <!-- SCANSION KEY: stressed lifts in CAPS in each line's audit comment. Stave = [letter: pattern];
>      "|" in the pattern is the caesura (aa|ax), distinct from the verse-text mark " || ". Tension tags:
>      {opposition | privation | causation | like-on-like | inversion: word ↔ word}; the unalliterated
>      final lift claims nothing and takes no tag. Pronunciation commitments:
>        Arimaspians = a-ri-MAS-pi-ans  → stave on /m/ (MAS); the word stands as an unalliterated lift
>        Demogorgon  = de-mo-GOR-gon    → stave on /g/ (GOR), NOT /d/
>        Confusion   = con-FU-sion      → stave on /f/ (FU), NOT /k/
>        Hades       = HAY-deez (/h/);  Orcus = OR-cus (vowel) -->
>
> <!-- COMMENTARY:
>    FIDELITY MAP. wilderness→"wilds" (L1). "winged course over hill or marshy dale"→"hill, hollow and
>    fen" (L2: hill=height, hollow/fen=the marshy low). The theft ("by stealth had stolen the guarded
>    gold") is given from the griffin's side as privation ("gold-reft", L1) plus the thieves' stealth
>    ("hid", L3), compressing Milton's narrated act into the bird's deprivation. "so eagerly the fiend"
>    →"so strains the Fiend" (strains carries "eagerly"). "o'er bog or steep, through strait, rough,
>    dense, or rare"→"slough and crag" (L4) + "clotted air" (L8, =dense); strait/rough/rare folded into
>    that variety of medium. Body catalog "head, hands, wings, or feet" split L5–L6; the five verbs
>    "swims or sinks, or wades, or creeps, or flies" survive intact across L6–L8 (wades L6; swims/sinks
>    L7; creeps + cleaves[=flies] L8). "universal uproar wild"→L9; "voices all confused"→"sundered
>    voices" (L10). "hollow dark"→"blind dark" (L11). "assaults his ear with loudest vehemence"→
>    "batters his ear" (L11): the intensifier is folded into the verb's violence rather than added as an
>    adjective, to obey the no-mood-pile rule. "power or spirit … nethermost abyss"→"power … pit of
>    night" (L13, "spirit" folded into "power"). "nearest coast of darkness … bordering on light"→
>    "which wall of the waste wanes nearest to light" (L15: coast=wall, bordering=wanes-to). The named
>    figures appear in Milton's order—Chaos, Night, Orcus, Hades, Demogorgon, Rumor, Chance, Tumult,
>    Confusion, Discord—across L16–L27. "the dreaded name of Demogorgon"→"a groan unguessed,
>    Demogorgon's name" (the dread IS the unspeakability). "all embroiled" kept (L26). "a thousand
>    various mouths"→"its din a thousand mouths" (L27, "various"=the din's multiplicity).
>    KENNING LEDGER. "gold-reft" (L1): privation—the griffin named by the gold taken from it.
>    "sunless deep" (L7): privation—the abyss defined by the light it lacks. "blind dark" (L11):
>    privation/inversion—dark granted the incapacity of sight. "clotted air" (L8): the medium as
>    curdled matter (=Milton's "dense"). "groan unguessed" (L22): privation—Demogorgon's name as an
>    unspoken sound, not a word. "chartless road" (L24): privation—Chance choosing with no chart.
>    This translation rides on verb-staves rather than nominal compounds (one kenning-pile would be
>    licensed in the court set-piece, but Milton's syntax there is itself verb-light, so the staves
>    argue through Hades-hoards, Tumult-tears, Confusion-founders, Discord-deafens instead). -->
>
> <div class="poem">
> As a griffin, gold-reft, || grinds through the wilds, <!-- 2+2: GRIFfin GOLD-reft | GRINDS wilds [g: aa|ax] {privation: griffin ↔ gold; verb=grinds} --> \
> heaves over hill, || over hollow and fen, <!-- 2+2: HEAVES HILL | HOLLow FEN [h: aa|ax] {causation: heaves ↔ hill; bonus opposition hill ↔ hollow} --> \
> and hounds them hard, || the hid Arimaspians—<!-- 2+2: HOUNDS HARD | HID a-ri-MAS-pians [h: aa|ax] {opposition: hounds ↔ hid (open chase vs hidden thief); verb=hounds; "Arimaspians" /m/, unalliterated final lift} --> \
> so strains the Fiend || through slough and crag, <!-- 2+2: STRAINS fiend | SLOUGH crag [s: ax|ax] {causation: strains ↔ slough (effort through bog); "Fiend" & "crag" unalliterated} --> \
> with head he heaves, || with hand he hauls, <!-- 2+2: HEAD HEAVES | HAND HAULS [h: aa|aa] {causation: head ↔ heaves, hand ↔ hauls (limbs as agents)}—DEVIATION: 4th lift staves; set-piece doubling, distinct verbs --> \
> he wings, he wades, || on weary foot, <!-- 2+2: WINGS WADES | WEARy FOOT [w: aa|ax] {opposition: wings ↔ wades (fly vs slog); "foot"=Milton's "feet"} --> \
> he swims or sinks || in the sunless deep, <!-- 2+2: SWIMS SINKS | SUNless DEEP [s: aa|ax] {opposition: swims ↔ sinks (float vs drown)} --> \
> creeps, then cleaves || the clotted air. <!-- 2+2: CREEPS CLEAVES | CLOTTed AIR [k: aa|ax] {opposition: creeps ↔ cleaves (crawl vs fly); "cleaves the air"=Milton's "flies"} --> \
> At length an uproar, || unchecked, overrides the dark, <!-- 2+2: UProar unCHECKED | OVERrides DARK [vowel: aa|ax] {agency: uproar ↔ overrides (din masters the void); "unchecked"=universal/wild} --> \
> stunning sounds || and sundered voices, <!-- 2+2: STUNNing SOUNDS | SUNDered VOICES [s: aa|ax] {causation: stunning ↔ sundered (the din that breaks the voices); "sundered"=confused} --> \
> born of the blind dark, || it batters his ear. <!-- 2+2: BORN BLIND | BATTers EAR [b: aa|ax] {agency: batters ↔ ear (sound strikes the body); "batters" carries "assaults"+"loudest vehemence"} --> \
> he plies toward the peal, || to probe, undaunted, <!-- 2+2: PLIES PEAL | PROBE undaunted [p: aa|ax] {agency: plies ↔ peal (he moves toward the noise); "undaunted" unalliterated} --> \
> what power presides || in the pit of night, <!-- 2+2: POWer preSIDES | PIT night [p: aa|ax] {causation: power ↔ presides (the entity that rules the abyss); "pit of night"=nethermost abyss} --> \
> and dwells in that din, || to demand the way out: <!-- 2+2: DWELLS DIN | deMAND out [d: aa|ax] {inversion: dwells ↔ din (din made a dwelling-place); verb=demand=Milton's "ask"} --> \
> which wall of the waste || wanes nearest to light. <!-- 2+2: WALL WASTE | WANES light [w: aa|ax] {opposition: waste ↔ light, the border; "wanes" enacts dark thinning into light; "nearest" preserved} --> \
> Then lo, Chaos keeps court, || his canopy spread <!-- 2+2: CHAOS COURT | CANopy spread [k: aa|ax] {causation: Chaos ↔ court (sovereign holds his realm)}—DEVIATION: stative reveal, verb off-stave ("keeps"/"spread"); see FORM --> \
> wide on the waste, || a world of dark deep; <!-- 2+2: WIDE WASTE | WORLD deep [w: aa|ax] {opposition: wide ↔ waste (the pavilion's span over the void)}—DEVIATION: stative tableau, verb off-stave --> \
> the eldest of ought, || in ebon, Night, <!-- 2+2: ELDest OUGHT | EBon NIGHT [vowel: aa|ax] {privation/age: eldest ↔ ought (older than aught=eldest of things); "ebon"=sable-vested; "Night" /n/ unalliterated final lift, named}—DEVIATION: stative tableau --> \
> his crowned consort; || the court closed round: <!-- 2+2: CROWNED CONsort | COURT CLOSED [k: aa|aa] {causation: crowned ↔ consort (he made her his queen); court ↔ closed=the throng gathering="by them stood"}—DEVIATION: 4th lift staves, set-piece --> \
> Orcus stood over them, || old as oaths, <!-- 2+2: ORcus OVer | OLD oaths [vowel: aa|ax] {causation/age: Orcus ↔ oaths (the death-god coeval with oaths sworn on the dead); verb=stood} --> \
> and Hades, who hoards || the hushed and dead, <!-- 2+2: HADes HOARDS | HUSHED dead [h: aa|ax] {causation: Hades ↔ hoards (the underworld hoards the silenced); verb=hoards} --> \
> a groan unguessed, || Demogorgon's name, <!-- 2+2: GROAN unGUESSED | de-mo-GORgon name [g: aa|ax] {privation: groan ↔ unguessed (the name unspoken, a sound not a word); "name" unalliterated} --> \
> Rumor next, that runs || and ravels the truth, <!-- 2+2: RUMor RUNS | RAVels truth [r: aa|ax] {agency: Rumor ↔ runs (abstraction as actor); ravels=tangles truth} --> \
> then Chance, that chooses || a chartless road, <!-- 2+2: CHANCE CHOOSes | CHARTless road [ch: aa|ax] {privation: Chance ↔ chartless (the chooser with no chart)} --> \
> Tumult that tears || and tugs at the deep, <!-- 2+2: TUMult TEARS | TUGS deep [t: aa|ax] {agency: Tumult ↔ tears (abstraction rends the void); two distinct verbs} --> \
> Confusion that founders, || all frayed, embroiled, <!-- 2+2: conFUsion FOUNDers | FRAYED emBROILED [f: aa|ax] {agency: Confusion ↔ founders (the merging that sinks); "embroiled"=Milton, "frayed" its gloss; "embroiled" /b/ unalliterated} --> \
> and Discord deafens, || its din a thousand mouths. <!-- 2+2: DIScord DEAFens | DIN mouths [d: aa|ax] {agency: Discord ↔ deafens (the many-mouthed din as assailant); "thousand mouths"=Milton} --> \
> </div>

## GPT-5.5 Pro {#gpt-5-5-pro-v2}

2026-06-11:

> <!-- FORM
> 2+2 lifts per verse line, divided by spaced || in the verse text.
> Preferred stave is aa|ax; ax|ax, ax|aa, and aa|aa appear where a proper name or final force-word needs the non-stave position.
> All vowels alliterate with all vowels, used for Arimaspians, ask, edge, opens, eldest, equal, owns, Orcus, unthroned, attended.
> Chaos, kept, king-throne, crowned, and cloaked use the k-sound stave.
> Hyphenated compounds normally count as one primary lift unless both halves are capped in the audit.
> Deliberate deviations are line-local in the audit comments.
> -->
> <!-- ARGUMENT ARC
> Paragraph 1: griffin simile, including wilderness flight, hill, marshy dale, Arimaspians, stealth, and guarded gold.
> Paragraph 2: Satanic crossing-catalog, bog, steep, strait, rough, dense, rare, body-instruments, and swim-sink-wade-creep-fly sequence.
> Paragraph 3: universal uproar, stunning sounds, confused voices, hollow dark, assault on the ear, and loud vehemence.
> Paragraph 4: undaunted advance toward whatever power or spirit dwells in the abyssal noise, to ask the nearest dark border with light.
> Paragraph 5: throne of Chaos, dark pavilion on the wasteful deep, Night as eldest consort, then Orcus, Hades, Demogorgon, Rumor, Chance, Tumult, Confusion, and Discord.
> -->
> <!-- SCANSION KEY
> Per-line audit format: lift count, stressed lifts in CAPS, [stave: pattern], {tension-tag: word ↔ word}.
> Pattern notation uses single pipe, as in aa|ax; verse caesura uses spaced ||.
> Tension-tags: opposition, privation, causation, like-on-like, inversion, violence.
> Vowel means all stressed vowel-initial lifts share one stave.
> Pronunciations: Arimaspians = ar-i-MAS-pi-ans; Chaos = KAY-os; Orcus = OR-kus; Hades = HAY-deez; Demogorgon = de-mo-GOR-gon.
> -->
> <!-- COMMENTARY
> Fidelity map: Milton's griffin simile becomes lines 1-5; wilderness, winged course, hills, marshy dale, Arimaspians, stealth, and guarded gold are all retained. Satan's eager passage becomes lines 6-13; bog, steep, strait, rough, dense, rare, head, hands, wings, feet, swims, sinks, wades, creeps, and flies remain in order. The uproar becomes lines 14-19; universal scale is world-wide, stunning sounds become sound striking sound, confused voices are named, hollow dark hurls the howls, and the ear is battered with lashing loudness. His resolve and question become lines 20-25; undauntedness is Dread cannot daunt, power and spirit are separated, the nethermost abyss is pit-bottom and sunk abyss, and the coast of darkness bordering on light is edge plus limit. The court of Chaos becomes lines 26-37; Chaos, throne, pavilion, wasteful deep, Night, eldest consort status, Orcus, Hades, Demogorgon, Rumor, Chance, Tumult, Confusion, and Discord appear in Milton's order. Compression: "nearest coast of darkness" is split into edge and limit so the boundary can alliterate as action; "thousand various mouths" becomes "thousand-mouthed, divided speech" so the variation is enacted rather than merely counted.
> Kenning ledger: gold-thieves binds pursuer to theft; dale-mire makes marshy dale an active drag; sentried gold ties treasure to the guard it loses; famished haste makes eagerness an appetite; broken steep makes ascent a damaged resistance; roughness makes terrain the attacker; world-wide keeps universal scale without Latin abstraction; pit-bottom din turns nethermost place into sound; sunk abyss makes depth a swallowed state; king-throne makes rule the thing Chaos unnaturally keeps; dark pavilion makes darkness architectural; waste-deep makes the wasteful deep a support; cloaked Night makes concealment her courtly function; star-shorn defines Night by the stars she lacks; sable-vested retains Milton's dark clothing as cover; eldest and equal binds age to consort-rank; unthroned marks Orcus and Hades as standing powers, not enthroned rulers; Demogorgon's name keeps dread attached to name rather than body; thousand-mouthed disperses Discord into many mouths.
> -->
> <div class="poem">
> A griffin grips || the gold-thieves' trail, <!-- 2+2: GRIFFIN GRIPS || GOLD-THIEVES TRAIL [g: aa|ax] {violence: griffin ↔ gold-thieves} --> \
> his wings whip || the waste over hills, <!-- 2+2: WINGS WHIP || WASTE HILLS [w: aa|ax] {violence: wings ↔ waste} --> \
> dale-mire drags || yet he drives on, <!-- 2+2: DALE-MIRE DRAGS || DRIVES ON [d: aa|ax] {opposition: mire ↔ drives} --> \
> Arimaspians, unseen, || entered the guard, <!-- 2+2: ARIMASPIANS UNSEEN || ENTERED GUARD [vowel: aa|ax] {causation: unseen ↔ entered} --> \
> stole by stealth || the sentried gold. <!-- 2+2: STOLE STEALTH || SENTRIED GOLD [s: aa|ax] {violence: stole ↔ sentried} --> \
>
> So the fiend follows || with famished haste, <!-- 2+2: FIEND FOLLOWS || FAMISHED HASTE [f: aa|ax] {causation: famished ↔ follows} --> \
> over bog he batters || up broken steep, <!-- 2+2: BOG BATTERS || BROKEN STEEP [b: aa|ax] {violence: batters ↔ bog} --> \
> through straits that strain || he strikes through rough, <!-- 2+2: STRAITS STRAIN || STRIKES ROUGH [s: aa|ax] {opposition: straits ↔ strikes} --> \
> roughness rakes him || through rare and dense, <!-- 2+2: ROUGHNESS RAKES || RARE DENSE [r: aa|ax] {violence: roughness ↔ rakes} --> \
> with head and hands || he hunts the way, <!-- 2+2: HEAD HANDS || HUNTS WAY [h: aa|ax] {causation: hands ↔ hunts} --> \
> wings wrestle || the way for feet, <!-- 2+2: WINGS WRESTLE || WAY FEET [w: aa|ax] {opposition: wings ↔ way} --> \
> he swims, then sinks || through swamp, then wades, <!-- 2+2: SWIMS SINKS || SWAMP WADES [s: aa|ax] {like-on-like: swims ↔ sinks} --> \
> then creeps, then cuts || the cloud in flight. <!-- 2+2: CREEPS CUTS || CLOUD FLIGHT [c: aa|ax] {opposition: cuts ↔ cloud} --> \
>
> A wildness wakes || world-wide uproar, <!-- 2+2: WILDNESS WAKES || WORLD-WIDE UPROAR [w: aa|ax] {causation: wildness ↔ wakes} --> \
> sound strikes || sound into voice, <!-- 2+2: SOUND STRIKES || SOUND VOICE [s: aa|ax] {like-on-like: sound ↔ sound} --> \
> voices vex || each voice confused, <!-- 2+2: VOICES VEX || VOICE CONFUSED [v: aa|ax] {like-on-like: voices ↔ voice} --> \
> through hollow dark || howls are hurled, <!-- 2+2: HOLLOW DARK || HOWLS HURLED [h: ax|aa] {inversion: hollow ↔ howls} --> \
> it beats through black || and batters his ear, <!-- 2+2: BEATS BLACK || BATTERS EAR [b: aa|ax] {inversion: black ↔ batters} --> \
> loudness lunges || with lashing force. <!-- 2+2: LOUDNESS LUNGES || LASHING FORCE [l: aa|ax] {inversion: loudness ↔ lunges} --> \
>
> Dread cannot daunt || him; he drives onward, <!-- 2+2: DREAD DAUNT || DRIVES ONWARD [d: aa|ax] {opposition: dread ↔ drives} --> \
> he meets what might || is making that noise, <!-- 2+2: MEETS MIGHT || MAKING NOISE [m: aa|ax] {causation: might ↔ making} --> \
> what power pulses || in pit-bottom din, <!-- 2+2: POWER PULSES || PIT-BOTTOM DIN [p: aa|ax] {causation: power ↔ pulses} --> \
> what spirit speaks || from sunk abyss, <!-- 2+2: SPIRIT SPEAKS || SUNK ABYSS [s: aa|ax] {causation: spirit ↔ speaks} --> \
> to ask what edge || opens nearest, <!-- 2+2: ASK EDGE || OPENS NEAREST [vowel: aa|ax] {opposition: edge ↔ opens} --> \
> where light lays || its limit on darkness. <!-- 2+2: LIGHT LAYS || LIMIT DARKNESS [l: aa|ax] {opposition: light ↔ darkness} --> \
>
> Then Chaos kept || his king-throne in void, <!-- 2+2: CHAOS KEPT || KING-THRONE VOID [k: aa|ax] {opposition: Chaos ↔ king-throne} --> \
> his dark pavilion || drove over depth, <!-- 2+2: DARK PAVILION || DROVE DEPTH [d: ax|aa] {inversion: pavilion ↔ drove} --> \
> waste-deep wore || its wide walls. <!-- 2+2: WASTE-DEEP WORE || WIDE WALLS [w: aa|aa] {inversion: waste-deep ↔ wore} --> \
> With Chaos crowned || sat cloaked Night, <!-- 2+2: CHAOS CROWNED || CLOAKED NIGHT [k: aa|ax] {causation: Chaos ↔ crowned} --> \
> star-shorn sat || sable-vested Night, <!-- 2+2: STAR-SHORN SAT || SABLE-VESTED NIGHT [s: aa|ax] {privation: star-shorn ↔ sable-vested} --> \
> eldest and equal || she owned his reign. <!-- 2+2: ELDEST EQUAL || OWNED REIGN [vowel: aa|ax] {causation: equal ↔ owned} --> \
> Orcus, unthroned, || attended Hades, <!-- 2+2: ORCUS UNTHRONED || ATTENDED HADES [vowel: aa|ax] {privation: unthroned ↔ attended} --> \
> and dread drew || Demogorgon's name. <!-- 2+2: DREAD DREW || DEMOGORGON NAME [d: aa|ax] {inversion: dread ↔ name} --> \
> Rumor ran next || raking up Chance, <!-- 2+2: RUMOR RAN || RAKING CHANCE [r: aa|ax] {causation: Rumor ↔ raking} --> \
> Tumult tangled || tight Confusion, <!-- 2+2: TUMULT TANGLED || TIGHT CONFUSION [t: aa|ax] {like-on-like: Tumult ↔ tangled} --> \
> all embroiled || in each other's noise, <!-- 2+2: ALL EMBROILED || EACH NOISE [vowel: aa|ax] {like-on-like: each ↔ other} --> \
> Discord, thousand-mouthed, || divided speech. <!-- 2+2: DISCORD THOUSAND-MOUTHED || DIVIDED SPEECH [d: ax|ax] {like-on-like: Discord ↔ divided} --> \
> </div>

## Claude-5-Fable {#claude-5-fable-v2}

2026-06-11:

<!-- FORM:
     4 lifts per line, 2+2 about the marked caesura; ll. 9 and 11 run 2+3, guardrailed inline.
     Stave patterns achieved: aa|ax ×11, ax|ax ×12, xa|ax ×2; deliberate deviations at l. 5 (xa|aa), l. 11 (no cross-stave), l. 28 (xa|aa), each guardrailed on the offending line.
     All vowels alliterate mutually; classical-name license invoked at l. 3 (Arimaspian) and l. 24 (either/Orcus).
     Cluster rule: st- alliterates only with st- (ll. 4, 7, 22); sh- (l. 16) and th- /θ/ (l. 19) are independent onsets, distinct from s- and t-.
     No end-rhyme: the incidental light/Night pair (ll. 17/21) was caught and removed in revision (light → day). -->

<!-- ARGUMENT ARC:
     vp 1 (ll. 1–4): griffin simile—the robbed guardian in pursuit.

<div class="poem">
> As the griffin, gold-robbed, || goes through the wilds, <!-- 2+2: GRIF-fin GOLD-robbed || GOES WILDS [g: aa|ax] {privation: griffin ↔ gold-robbed—the guardian defined by his loss, and the loss drives the verb} --> \

> and aims his ire || at the Arimaspian thieves <!-- 2+2: AIMS IRE || AR-imaspian THIEVES [vowel: aa|ax] {causation: aimed ire ↔ Arimaspians—wrath bound to its target; AR- per scansion key} --> \
> who stole by stealth || the strong-watched gold—<!-- 2+2: STOLE STEALTH || STRONG-watched GOLD [st: aa|ax] {opposition: stealth ↔ strong-watch—theft against guard; stole/stealth cognate, theft squared} --> \
> so storms the fiend: || the fear-smith fares <!-- 2+2: STORMS FIEND || FEAR-smith FARES [f: xa|aa] {causation: fiend ↔ fear-smith—woe as manufacture} guardrail: final lift alliterates; the triple f chains devil→product→motion, momentum overrunning the line-end --> \
> over bluff, over bog, || through brake and clearing; <!-- 2+2: BLUFF BOG || BRAKE CLEAR-ing [b: aa|ax] {opposition: bluff ↔ bog—high dry against low wet; brake ↔ clearing—Milton's dense and rare as thicket and open ground} --> \
> through strait and rough || he stretches his way, <!-- 2+2: STRAIT ROUGH || STRETCH-es WAY [st: ax|ax] {opposition: strait ↔ stretch—the narrows squeeze, the body extends; the verb fights the noun} --> \
> with head, with hands, || with heel and wing: <!-- 2+2: HEAD HANDS || HEEL WING [h: aa|ax] {like-on-like: limb piled on limb, the whole anatomy conscripted to one motion—Miltonic catalog, sanctioned set-piece} --> \
> he swims, he wades, || he sinks, creeps, flies. <!-- 2+3: SWIMS WADES || SINKS CREEPS FLIES [s: ax|ax] {opposition: swims ↔ sinks—buoyancy against drowning} guardrail: fifth lift kept for Milton's verb-pentad; sinks/wades transposed for the cross-stave --> \
> Then a noise from nowhere, || all the nether voices, <!-- 2+2: NOISE NO-where || NE-ther VOI-ces [n: aa|ax] {privation: noise ↔ nowhere—a din with no author, sourced in absence} --> \
> sound against sound, || voice into voice broken, <!-- 2+3: SOUND SOUND || VOICE VOICE BRO-ken [s‖v: no cross-stave] {like-on-like ×2: noise destroying noise} guardrail: the halves refuse a common stave—the broken correspondence enacted; "broken" the one word the pattern cannot absorb --> \
> through the hollow it hails, || hammering his ear <!-- 2+2: HOL-low HAILS || HAM-mering EAR [h: aa|ax] {inversion: the hollow made hurler—emptiness precipitates assault} --> \
> with utmost violence. || Onward he turns, <!-- 2+2: UT-most VI-olence || ON-ward TURNS [vowel: ax|ax] {opposition: violence ↔ onward—assault answered by advance} --> \
> undaunted to meet || whatever dwells in the noise, <!-- 2+2: un-DAUNT-ed MEET || DWELLS NOISE [d: ax|ax] {privation: undaunted ↔ dwells—the fearless one seeking fear's own address} --> \
> power or phantom || of the pit's last floor, <!-- 2+2: POW-er PHAN-tom || PIT'S FLOOR [p: aa|ax] {opposition: power ↔ phantom—substance or shade, the doubt of what waits} --> \
> and ask the shortest way || to the shore of the dark <!-- 2+2: ASK SHORT-est || SHORE DARK [sh: ax|ax] {tension: shortest ↔ shore—the quickest end of an endless place; darkness granted a coastline} --> \
> where dark gives ground || to the grey of day. <!-- 2+2: DARK GROUND || GREY DAY [g: xa|ax] {opposition: dark's retreat bound to dawn's advance—the border as event} --> \
> When lo—the lord || of no law, Chaos, <!-- 2+2: LO LORD || no-LAW CHA-os [l: aa|ax] {privation: lord ↔ no-law—sovereign of statutelessness, Milton's Anarch} --> \
> throned in a tent || of thickest shadow <!-- 2+2: THRONED TENT || THICK-est SHA-dow [th /θ/: ax|ax] {causation: throned ↔ thickest—his seat is built of murk; tent against throne, permanence claimed by canvas} --> \
> spread wide on the waste || where no world is; <!-- 2+2: WIDE WASTE || WORLD IS [w: aa|ax] {privation: waste ↔ world—the deep defined by the creation it lacks} --> \
> enthroned beside him || sat sable-swathed Night, <!-- 2+2: en-THRONED be-SIDE || SA-ble-swathed NIGHT [s: xa|ax] {causation: side ↔ sable—her seat and her swathing one estate; consortship conducted in black} --> \
> star-shorn, the eldest || of all standing things, <!-- 2+2: STAR-shorn ELD-est || STAND-ing THINGS [st: ax|ax] {privation: star-shorn ↔ standing—stripped of her stars, senior to all that stands} --> \
> old as oblivion, || his anguished queen; <!-- 2+2: OLD ob-LIV-ion || AN-guished QUEEN [vowel: ax|ax] {tension: old ↔ anguished—her antiquity is her grief; coeval with forgetting} --> \
> and on either hand stood || Orcus and Hades <!-- 2+2: EI-ther HAND || OR-cus HA-des [vowel: ax|ax] {inversion: hell's kings made flanking attendants—death demoted to courtier} --> \
> and the dreaded name || of Demogorgon; <!-- 2+2: DREAD-ed NAME || DE-mo-GOR-gon [d: ax|ax] {causation: dread ↔ Demogorgon—fear manufactured by naming; the name attends where the named does not; DEE- per scansion key} --> \
> Rumor next ran by || with reckless Chance; <!-- 2+2: RU-mor RAN || RECK-less CHANCE [r: aa|ax] {inversion: report made runner; privation: reckless ↔ Chance—chance is the absence of care} --> \
> Tumult and Confusion, || trampling each other; <!-- 2+2: TU-mult con-FU-sion || TRAM-pling OTH-er [t: ax|ax] {like-on-like: the rioters riot on themselves—"embroiled" enacted} --> \
> and thousand-mouthed Discord, || maw against maw. <!-- 2+2: THOU-sand-MOUTHED DIS-cord || MAW MAW [m: xa|aa] {like-on-like: discord self-divided, every mouth at war with its twin} guardrail: final lift alliterates—the mirror must mirror -->
</div>

# v3

<!-- Perished Paradise prompt, v3 (2026-06-11). Changes from v2: fixed Stage-3 cross-reference bug ((1)→(2)); fixed {.peom} typo; added relation 6 "violence"; exemplar quarantine (no verbatim reuse of exemplar phrasing); anti-confabulation test for tension tags + Draft-B FAIL quota; ledger ↔ text two-way verification, delivery-blocking; graceful-degradation clause (honest FAIL tags > refusal or fake compliance); soft length cap (≤1.3× source lines); optional strict-cluster rule (st-/sp-/sc- self-alliterating; sh-/th- independent onsets); standardized notation (single-pipe `aa|ax` in audit comments versus spaced ` || ` verse caesura). -->

Translate the passage of John Milton's _Paradise Lost_ given at the end of this prompt into modern English alliterative verse on the Old English model. The project is dubbed _Perished Paradise_: a glimpse of the alliterative ur-epic Milton might have written had _Genesis B_, not Virgil, been his formal master.

## Form

- 4 stressed syllables per line, divided by a marked caesura (` || `).
- Alliteration binds stressed syllables across the caesura. Prefer the classical `aa|ax` pattern: both lifts of the first half-line and the first lift of the second half-line alliterate; the final lift does not. `ax|ax` is acceptable; a line whose halves share no stave is a defect.
- **Notation:** the single-pipe form (`aa|ax`) is reserved for pattern notation inside audit comments; the spaced ` || ` is the caesura mark in verse text. Never interchange them.
- Alliteration is by the initial sound of the stressed syllable. All vowels alliterate with one another (exploit this for classical names: Orcus / awe / ash).
- **Cluster rule (recommended; declare your choice in the FORM block).** The clusters st-, sp-, and sc-/sk- alliterate only with themselves, never with plain s-; sh- and th- are independent onsets, distinct from s- and t-. This is authentic Old English practice and costs little. If you decline it, say so in FORM rather than silently mixing onsets.
- No end-rhyme. If rhyme creeps in incidentally, remove it: rhyme is the gravity well this form must escape.
- Modern English vocabulary throughout. No Old English words (no _wyrd_, _nið_, _sæ-_), no faux-archaism ("'twas," "doth"; "o'er" tolerated sparingly). The intended reader knows neither Old English nor Milton; the poem must stand alone and give pleasure unassisted.

## Fidelity

This is translation, not imitation. Every substantive element of Milton's passage must survive recognizably: similes with their referents intact, verb catalogs at full strength, named figures present and in order, the narrative sequence unbroken. Rearrange syntax and lineation freely; compress connective tissue; do not drop content or invent episodes.

**Length cap.** Target at most 1.3× the source's line count. Amplification is permitted only when it serves a tension (eg. doubling the theft into the victim's epithet), and every amplification must be documented in the fidelity map. Padding to feed the stave is a fidelity failure, not a formal success.

## The cardinal rule: alliteration must argue

In Old English verse, alliteration is syntax, not ornament: the stave yokes the two most important words of the line and asserts a relation between them. Your alliterating words must stand in semantic tension—they must argue, not agree. The acceptable relations:

1. **Opposition.** The stave binds antagonists, so the sound enacts the border between them.
2. **Privation.** An epithet defined by lack; the stave ties the subject to the very thing it is denied.
3. **Causation / craft.** Agent bound to product; an abstraction presented as deliberate manufacture.
4. **Violence of like upon like.** Repetition enacting the self-destruction it describes.
5. **Agency inversion.** An abstraction made the actor, a body or person made the patient.
6. **Violence.** Asymmetric force: one stave-word strikes, drags, or breaks the other ("wings whip the waste"). Distinct from like-on-like in that the combatants differ; the stave binds assailant to victim.

**Forbidden: alliterating synonyms or mood-twins.** ["Grim griffin glides through gloomy glens"]{.poem} contains four words that all mean "dark and scary"; the line asserts nothing and FAILS no matter how many staves it carries. One mood-word per stave, maximum. A line with two arguing staves beats a line with five agreeing ones.

**Forbidden: grammar masquerading as tension.** A claimed relation must survive this check: would it be equally true of any two words in an ordinary sentence? "{causation: griffin ↔ grips—the act defines the hunter}" fails the check, because every agent is "defined by" its verb; that is grammar, not tension. So is "perception bound to its object" for any verb of seeing. If the only honest tag is `{none}`, write `{none}` and rewrite the line; a confabulated tag is worse than an honest defect, because it corrupts the audit trail that justifies the whole apparatus.

**Verbs must carry staves.** In each line, at least one alliterating position should be a finite verb or verb-derived word, and that verb should do something *to* another alliterating word. Catalogs of compound nouns (["_X_-_Y_ noun || _Z_-_W_ noun"]{.poem}) are permitted at most once per 8 lines, reserved for formal set-pieces such as the court of Chaos—and even there, the compounds must each pass the tension test.

## Exemplar lines

**Quarantine.** The exemplars below are drawn from prior translations of this very passage. They demonstrate the standard; they are not material. Reusing their phrasing, epithets, or close variants in your translation FAILS the line—invent your own tensions. Some exemplars also predate the strict stress-count above; imitate their stave-logic, not their meter.

Study these before drafting. Each is annotated with the tension its stave asserts; this annotation format is the one you will apply to your own lines.

<div class="poem">
> to ask where mirk-marches meet morning, where dark \
> gives ground to the gleam, at the grey verge of light
</div>

— Opposition. The m-stave and g-stave each bind darkness to the daylight it borders; "gives ground" puts the boundary's own action on the stave—dark literally retreats. The alliteration *is* the border being described.

<div class="poem">
> Sound against sound, voice into voice broken
</div>

— Violence of like upon like. The repetition performs the self-destruction it reports; "broken" lands as the unalliterated final lift, the one word the pattern cannot absorb.

<div class="poem">
> Sable-swathed Night, star-shorn, first-born
</div>

— Privation. The s-stave ties Night to the stars she lacks; she is defined by deprivation, and the sound will not let her escape it.

<div class="poem">
> Old as oblivion, his anguished queen
</div>

— Vowel-stave binding age to erasure: to be as old as oblivion is to be coeval with forgetting. "Anguished" gives the personification interiority rather than costume.

<div class="poem">
> By her the hush held and the heart grew cold
</div>

— Agency inversion. The h-stave makes silence the actor and the heart the patient; an absence is granted grip.

<div class="poem">
> So storms the fiend. The fear-smith fares
</div>

— Causation/craft: "fear-smith" presents woe as deliberate manufacture, and the verbs "storms"/"fares" carry their staves rather than leaving the work to adjectives.

Counter-exemplar, for calibration: ["Doom-depths dire now drive dread-sounds deep"]{.poem}—6 staves, zero claims; every word restates "scary"; the verb is inert. FAIL.

## Procedure

Work in one pass through three internal stages. Show all three.

**Stage 1—Draft A (sense-first).** Translate the passage into the form above, prioritizing syntax, clarity, and narrative momentum. Alliterate where it comes naturally; never wrench word order for a stave. This draft establishes what the lines must *say*.

**Stage 2—Draft B (sound-first).** Re-translate, now maximizing sound-density: kennings, compounds, heavy staves, appositive variation. Push past good taste—most of this draft will be cut. Draft B exists only to generate candidate sound-effects for the hybrid; it is ore, not metal.

**Stage 3—Audit and hybrid.** Apply the following test to every line of both drafts, then compose the final hybrid keeping Draft A's syntax and momentum and importing from Draft B only those effects that pass:

For each line, state:

#. which words carry the staves;
#. what claim the binding makes—which relation of opposition, privation, causation, like-on-like, inversion, or violence the sound asserts;
#. PASS or FAIL. If the honest answer to (2) is "none—the words merely share a mood or restate each other," the line FAILS regardless of how many alliterations it contains, and must be rewritten so that at least one stave falls on a verb or on a pair of words in tension.

**Calibration check:** your audit of Draft B should FAIL at least a third of its lines. Draft B was written past good taste; if your audit passes nearly everything, your audit is too lenient, not your draft too good.

Re-audit the hybrid and revise until every line passes, or until you can state in one clause why a FAIL is deliberately retained. **Anti-loop rule:** every audit cycle must change the text or terminate with that justification; never emit an unchanged version and call it improved. Three cycles should suffice; endless polishing is itself a failure mode.

**Graceful degradation.** Full compliance is not always possible—Milton's five-verb catalog cannot stave completely, and some proper names resist every pattern. When constraints conflict, deliver the poem with honest FAIL tags and inline guardrails rather than refusing the task or fabricating compliance. An honestly documented deviation is conformant; a confabulated tag is not. The audit trail, not perfection, is the deliverable.

## Deliverable

Format the final poem for direct pasting into a Gwern.net source file, following its poetry conventions exactly:

1. **Poem block.** Wrap the poem in `<div class="poem">`{.HTML}. Every verse line ends with ` \`{.Markdown}, and the backslash must be the *last character on the line*—all HTML comments therefore go *before* the backslash, never after. Mark the caesura with ` || `{.Markdown} (space, double-pipe, space). Separate verse paragraphs with a single blank line.
2. **Header comment blocks**, placed immediately before the poem block, in this order:

   - `<!-- FORM ... -->`{.HTML}—the metrical scheme actually achieved: stress counts per half-line, stave-pattern preference (`aa|ax`), cluster-rule choice (adopted or declined), vowel-alliteration license for classical names, and every deliberate deviation, each in one line.
   - `<!-- ARGUMENT ARC ... -->`{.HTML}—what each verse paragraph does in the narrative progression (eg. simile → crossing-catalog → uproar → resolve → court of Chaos).
   - `<!-- SCANSION KEY ... -->`{.HTML}—defines the per-line audit format below: stress-casing convention (stressed syllables in CAPS), stave notation (single-pipe `aa|ax` in audit comments, never the spaced verse caesura), tension-tag vocabulary (opposition | privation | causation | like-on-like | inversion | violence), and pronunciation commitments for any unstable words (classical names especially: eg. `Demogorgon = de-mo-GOR-gon`).
   - `<!-- COMMENTARY ... -->`{.HTML}—two parts. **Fidelity map:** where each Miltonic element went (simile sources, verb catalogs, order of named figures), the reasoning for any compression, and every amplification per the length cap. **Kenning ledger:** every compound and epithet retained in the final text, each justified in one clause by the tension it asserts; any kenning you cannot justify in one clause must be cut before delivery, not excused after it.
   - **Verification (delivery-blocking).** Every kenning-ledger entry and every illustrative example in FORM must quote a phrase present verbatim in the final poem, with its line number. Before delivery, check the correspondence both ways: a ledger entry with no matching line, or a FORM example quoting words the poem does not contain, is a delivery-blocking error, not a footnote.
3. **Per-line audit comments.** Each verse line carries a post-comment (before the backslash) compressing its Stage-3 audit into one line of mechanical notation—stressed lifts in CAPS, stave letter and pattern in brackets, tension tag in braces:

   ~~~{.HTML}
   <!-- 2+2: LIFT LIFT || LIFT lift [stave: pattern] {tension: word ↔ word} -->
   ~~~

   Worked example (the line is from the quarantined exemplars; it illustrates the comment format only):

   ~~~{.HTML}
   Sable-swathed Night, || star-shorn, first-born, <!-- 2+2: SA-ble NIGHT || STAR-SHORN BORN [s: aa|ax] {privation: Night ↔ stars} --> \
   ~~~

   The `{tension}` tag names which relation (opposition | privation | causation | like-on-like | inversion | violence) the stave asserts, and between which words. The unalliterated final lift claims nothing and needs no tag of its own. A line whose tag would honestly read `{none}` should not have survived Stage 3; if one is retained anyway (eg. for fidelity), say so in the same comment.
4. **Inline guardrails.** Deliberate rule violations—an extra lift, a mood-word pair kept for fidelity, an unusual word choice—are documented in an HTML comment on the offending line itself, not summarized elsewhere.

## The passage

<!-- Deliberate choice: this is the modernized griffin passage used in every prior run (Gwern & GPT-4 2023), not Milton's 1674 orthography ("Gryfon ... moarie Dale ... universal hubbub"), retained for cross-run comparability. -->

<div class="poem">
"As when a griffin through the wilderness, \
With winged course over hill or marshy dale, \
Pursues the Arimaspians, who by stealth \
Had stolen the guarded gold, so eagerly the fiend \
O'er bog or steep, through strait, rough, dense, or rare, \
With head, hands, wings, or feet pursues his way, \
And swims or sinks, or wades, or creeps, or flies. \
At length, a universal uproar wild \
Of stunning sounds and voices all confused, \
Born through the hollow dark, assaults his ear \
With loudest vehemence. Thither he plies, \
Undaunted to meet whatever power or spirit \
Of the nethermost abyss might dwell in that noise, \
Of whom to ask which way the nearest coast of darkness lies, \
Bordering on light. When lo, the throne \
Of Chaos and his dark pavilion spread \
Wide on the wasteful deep; with him enthroned \
Sat sable-vested Night, eldest of things, \
The consort of his reign, and by them stood \
Orcus and Hades, and the dreaded name \
Of Demogorgon; Rumor next, and Chance, \
And Tumult and Confusion, all embroiled, \
And Discord with a thousand various mouths."
</div>
