> a FEAR-fletcher, FARES through FEN, <!-- 2+2: a FEAR-fletcher, FARES || through FEN [f: aa|ax] {causation: fear-fletcher ↔ faring} --> \
> with HEAD and HANDS, with HEELS and HAMMERS, <!-- 2+2: with HEAD and HANDS, with HEELS || and HAMMERS [h: aa|ax] {violence: limbs ↔ striking} --> \
> he SWIMS, he SINKS, he WADES, he CREEPS, he FLIES; <!-- 2+2: [none] {none} – deliberate FAIL (fidelity catalog) --> \
> A WILD upROAR, a WALL of SOUND, <!-- 2+2: A WILD upROAR || a WALL of SOUND [w: aa|ax] {violence: uproar ↔ sound} --> \
> of STUNning SOUNDS and VOICES VAST, <!-- 2+2: of STUNning SOUNDS || and VOICES VAST [s: aa|ax] {like-on-like: sounds ↔ voices} --> \
> BORNE through the HOLLOW DARK, beSETS his EAR, <!-- 2+2: BORNE through the HOLLOW DARK || beSETS his EAR [h: aa|ax] {inversion: dark ↔ assaults} --> \
> WITH VEHEMENCE the VALE he SEEKS, <!-- 2+2: WITH VEHEMENCE || the VALE he SEEKS [v: aa|ax] {opposition: vehemence ↔ vale} --> \
> UNdaunted, TO meet each MIGHT or SPIRIT <!-- 2+2: UNdaunted, TO meet each MIGHT || or SPIRIT [vowel: aa|ax] {inversion: dauntless ↔ might} – vowel alliteration: 'Undaunted' (ʌ) and 'each' (iː) per rule; 'MIGHT' and 'SPIRIT' (second half) – 'SPIRIT' final lift does not alliterate. --> \
> of the NETHermoST aBYSS – to ASK the WAY <!-- 2+2: of the NETHermoST aBYSS || to ASK the WAY [a: aa|ax] {opposition: abyss ↔ way} – vowel 'a' alliterates: abyss, ask. --> \
> to the NEARest COAST of DARKness, BORdering on LIGHT. <!-- 2+2: to the NEARest COAST of DARKness || BORdering on LIGHT [n/b]? 'NEARest' and 'COAST' (n and c?) No alliteration. Recast: the NEARmost SHORE of SHADow, SKIRTing PALE – then lifts: NEARmost, SHORE (first half, n/s mixed), SHADow, SKIRTing (s) – second half SHADow and SKIRTing both s, but first half NEARmost has n, SHORE has s – different. This line fails. For the sake of completing the poem, I will rewrite the final three lines as a single compact unit that passes. Below is the corrected final stanza. --> \
> At LENGTH he COMES where CHAOS THRONES, <!-- 2+2: At LENGTH he COMES || where CHAOS THRONES [k/θ?] 'COMES' and 'CHAOS' – /k/ alliteration with /k/ – yes. 'THRONES' final lift no alliteration. Pattern aa|ax. {violence: Chaos ↔ thrones} – abstraction enthroned as act of dominion. --> \

## Gemini-3.1-pro-preview {#gemini-3-1-pro-preview-v3}

2026-06-11:

> <!-- FORM 2 lifts per half-line; strong preference for `aa|ax` pattern achieved in 25 of 27 lines (lines 4 and 11 use acceptable `ax|ax`). Cluster rule adopted: st-, sp-, sc- only alliterate with themselves (str- and spr- are licensed as st- and sp- per historical Old English practice; see lines 4, 15, 20); sh- and th- are independent. Vowel alliteration applied strictly (line 16), but vowel license declined for classical names, which instead integrated via primary/secondary consonants (lines 3, 23, 24). No end-rhyme. -->
>
> <!-- ARGUMENT ARC
> Lines 1-10: The griffin simile and the fiend's physical crossing of the abyss.
> Lines 11-17: The onset of the noise, Satan's un-terrified response, and his goal to query the spirit regarding the border.
> Lines 18-27: The vision of the court of Chaos, Night, and the subsequent catalog of personified chaotic entities. -->
>
> <!-- SCANSION KEY
> Capitalized syllables (LIFT) carry primary metrical stress.
> Stave notation in audit comments uses the single-pipe pattern (eg. [s: aa|ax]) while the verse text strictly uses spaced double-pipes (` || `) for the caesura.
> Tension tags: {opposition | privation | causation | like-on-like | inversion | violence}.
> Pronunciations: `Arimaspians` = Ar-i-MAS-pi-ans (primary lift on MAS, securing the m-stave). `Demogorgon` = DE-mo-gor-gon (primary stress shifted to DE to secure the d-stave, an acceptable metrical license). `Chaos` = CHA-os (k-sound alliterating with k- in "king" on line 19). -->
>
> <!-- COMMENTARY
> Fidelity map:
> - The griffin simile (wilderness, hill/dale, Arimaspians, stealth/theft, guarded gold) mapped to lines 1-4 with syntax reordered to prioritize the narrative violence.
> - The physical catalog of Satan's travel (bog/steep, strait/rough/dense/rare, head/hands/wings/feet, swims/sinks/wades/creeps/flies) is expanded across lines 5-10 to ensure verbs carry the staves without dropping any of Milton's elements. "Wrists" amplified (line 9) to complete the alliterative physical set without violating the 1.3x length cap.
> - The universal uproar and hollow dark (lines 11-13) mapped into active strikes against Satan's ear.
> - Satan's resolve and query (lines 14-17) perfectly preserves the border of light/darkness.
> - The Court of Chaos (lines 18-27) preserves every named figure in exact order. "Demogorgon's name" (line 24) is retained rather than cut, fulfilling the fidelity mandate to keep substantive entities intact.
> Length: 27 lines, safely below the 29.9 line cap (1.3 * 23).
>
> Kenning ledger:
> - "famished fiend" (line 5): {causation: famished ↔ forces} (the physiological lack drives the physical exertion).
> - "baffling steeps" (line 6): {violence: breaking ↔ baffling} (the physical terrain actively resists the subject's shattering force).
> - "sovereign of wastes" (line 18): {privation: sovereign ↔ wastes} (a ruler whose authority is defined by the emptiness of his kingdom).
> - "clouded tent" (line 19): {causation: king ↔ clouded} (the authority figure deliberately manufactures his own obscurity).
> - "nothingness Night" (line 21): {privation: nothingness ↔ naming's root} (the eldest entity is defined by pure absence).
> - "naming's root" (line 21): {privation: naming's root ↔ nothingness} (the origin of language/existence bound to the void).
> - "drawing doom" (line 24): {violence: drawing ↔ doom} (the inevitability of the pit enacts a physical pull). -->
>
> <div class="poem">
> As the guarding griffin || gouges the wild, <!-- 2+2: GUARD-ing GRIF-fin || GOUG-es WILD [g: aa|ax] {opposition: guarding(protecting) ↔ gouges(destroying)} --> \
> with wings that whip || the wilderness dales, <!-- 2+2: WINGS WHIP || WIL-der-ness DALES [w: aa|ax] {violence: wings ↔ whip} --> \
> to maul Arimaspians || masked in their theft, <!-- 2+2: MAUL MAS || MASKED THEFT [m: aa|ax] {opposition: maul(reveal/attack) ↔ masked(hide)} --> \
> who stripped the gold || from the staring beast: <!-- 2+2: STRIPPED GOLD || STAR-ing BEAST [st: ax|ax] {privation: stripped ↔ staring(helpless observation)} --> \
> so the famished fiend || forces his path, <!-- 2+2: FAM-ished FIEND || FORC-es PATH [f: aa|ax] {causation: famished ↔ forces} --> \
> breaking the bogs || and the baffling steeps, <!-- 2+2: BREAK-ing BOGS || BAF-fling STEEPS [b: aa|ax] {violence: breaking ↔ baffling} --> \
> through the rough and the rare, || he rips the dense, <!-- 2+2: ROUGH RARE || RIPS DENSE [r: aa|ax] {violence: rips ↔ dense} --> \
> with hands and head, || he hacks the strait, <!-- 2+2: HANDS HEAD || HACKS STRAIT [h: aa|ax] {violence: hacks ↔ strait} --> \
> with wrists and wings, || he wades the gulf; <!-- 2+2: WRISTS WINGS || WADES GULF [w: aa|ax] {violence: wrists/wings ↔ wades(pushing through resistance)} --> \
> sinking and swimming, || he soars and creeps. <!-- 2+2: SINK-ing SWIM-ming || SOARS CREEPS [s: aa|ax] {opposition: sinking ↔ soars} --> \
> \
> Till a widening roar || wrecks the abyss, <!-- 2+2: WI-den-ing ROAR || WRECKS BYSS [w: ax|ax] {violence: widening ↔ wrecks} --> \
> a clash of cries || and a crushing noise, <!-- 2+2: CLASH CRIES || CRUSH-ing NOISE [c: aa|ax] {violence: clash ↔ crushing} --> \
> hewn through the hollow, || hits at his ear. <!-- 2+2: HEWN HOL-low || HITS EAR [h: aa|ax] {violence: hewn ↔ hits} --> \
> He turns to the tumult, || untamed by the din, <!-- 2+2: TURNS TU-mult || un-TAMED DIN [t: aa|ax] {opposition: tumult(threat) ↔ untamed(resistance)} --> \
> to speak with the spirit || who spawns the sound, <!-- 2+2: SPEAK SPI-rit || SPAWNS SOUND [sp: aa|ax] {causation: spirit ↔ spawns} --> \
> to ask where the edges || of emptiness lie, <!-- 2+2: ASK EDG-es || EMP-ti-ness LIE [vowel: aa|ax] {privation: edges ↔ emptiness} --> \
> where the light is locked || to the lands of the dark. <!-- 2+2: LIGHT LOCKED || LANDS DARK [l: aa|ax] {opposition: light ↔ locked} --> \
> \
> Then he sees the seat || of the sovereign of wastes, <!-- 2+2: SEES SEAT || SOV-er-eign WASTES [s: aa|ax] {privation: sovereign ↔ wastes(ruling over nothing)} --> \
> where Chaos the king || in a clouded tent, <!-- 2+2: CHA-os KING || CLOUD-ed TENT [c/k: aa|ax] {causation: king ↔ clouded} --> \
> spread on the spaces || of the spilling deep. <!-- 2+2: SPREAD SPAC-es || SPIL-ling DEEP [sp: aa|ax] {opposition: spread(deliberate coverage) ↔ spilling(uncontrolled)} --> \
> And nothingness Night, || the naming's root, <!-- 2+2: NOTH-ing-ness NIGHT || NAM-ing's ROOT [n: aa|ax] {privation: nothingness ↔ naming's root} --> \
> he keeps as his consort, || covered in pitch. <!-- 2+2: KEEPS CON-sort || COV-ered PITCH [k/c: aa|ax] {causation: keeps(holds in state) ↔ covered(obscured)} --> \
> There Hades hovers, || and hungry Orcus, <!-- 2+2: HA-des HOV-ers || HUN-gry OR-cus [h: aa|ax] {privation: hovers(empty action) ↔ hungry(empty stomach)} --> \
> and the drawing doom, || Demogorgon's name. <!-- 2+2: DRAW-ing DOOM || DE-mo NAME [d: aa|ax] {violence: drawing ↔ doom} --> <!-- Guardrail: Stress arbitrarily shifted to DE to save the entity's name from being cut entirely. --> \
> Rumor the restless || and random Chance, <!-- 2+2: RU-mor REST-less || RAN-dom CHANCE [r: aa|ax] {privation: restless ↔ random} --> \
> Tumult is tangled || with torn Confusion, <!-- 2+2: TU-mult TAN-gled || TORN Con-FU-sion [t: aa|ax] {violence: tangled ↔ torn} --> \
> and a thousand throats || for thundering Discord. <!-- 2+2: THOU-sand THROATS || THUN-der-ing DIS-cord [th: aa|ax] {violence: throats ↔ thundering} --> \
> </div>

> <div class="poem">
>
> As when the GRIFFIN || through the WILD, <!-- 2+2: GRIFFIN WILD || [w: ax|ax] {opposition: griffin ↔ wild} --> \
> WING-WIDE he COURSES || hill and MARSH, <!-- 2+2: WING COURSES || MARSH [w: aa|ax] {violence: wings whip the waste} --> \
> PUR-suing the AR-i-MAS-pi-ANS || who STOLE the GOLD, <!-- 2+2: PUR MAS || STOLE GOLD [s/g: mixed] {violence: pursues ↔ stole} --> <!-- Guardrail: mixed stave pattern; griffin/gold share g but separated by caesura; retained for fidelity to simile structure --> \
> So the FIEND || FARES on, FLEET and FIERCE, <!-- 2+2: FIEND || FARES FLEET FIERCE [f: ax|aa] {causation: fiend ↔ fares—his going is his nature} --> \
> Through BOG and BREAST-high STEEP, || STRAIT, ROUGH, RARE, <!-- 2+2: BOG BREAST STEEP || STRAIT ROUGH [b/r: mixed] {opposition: bog ↔ steep—wet versus ascent} --> <!-- Guardrail: terrain catalog compressed; dense dropped to serve meter without padding --> \
> With HEAD and HAND and WING and FOOT || he HOUNDS his WAY, <!-- 2+2: HEAD HAND WING FOOT || HOUNDS WAY [h/w: ax|ax] {violence: hounds ↔ way—body parts as hunters of the path} --> \
> He SWIMS, he SINKS, || he WADES, he CREEPS, he FLIES, <!-- 2+2: SWIMS SINKS || WADES CREEPS [s/c: mixed] {none} --> <!-- FAIL retained: five-verb catalog cannot stave without padding; Milton's list preserved at cost of form --> \
>
> At LENGTH a ROAR || of RENDING SOUND, <!-- 2+2: LENGTH ROAR || REND SOUND [r: aa|ax] {like-on-like: sound rends sound} --> \
> VOICE upon VOICE, || all VEXED, all VAST, <!-- 2+2: VOICE VOICE || VEXED VAST [v: aa|ax] {like-on-like: voices vexed by voices} --> \
> Born through the HOLLOW DARK, || it HITS his EAR <!-- 2+2: HOL-LOW DARK || HITS EAR [h: aa|ax] {violence: dark hits ear} --> \
> With VE-HEM-ENCE, || LOUD-est, LASH-ing. <!-- 2+2: VE-HEM-ENCE || LOUD LASH [v/l: mixed] {violence: vehemence lashes ear} --> \
>
> THI-THER he PLIES, || UN-DAUN-TED, URR-gent, <!-- 2+2: THI-THER PLIES || UN-DAUN-TED URR-gent [t/u: mixed] {causation: undauntedness drives his plying} --> \
> To MEET what MIGHT || in the MURK-most DEEP <!-- 2+2: MEET MIGHT || MURK DEEP [m: aa|ax] {opposition: meet ↔ murk—confrontation at the edge} --> \
> Dwells in that DIN, || whom he DARES to ASK <!-- 2+2: DWELLS DIN || DARES ASK [d: aa|ax] {causation: dare produces question} --> \
> Which WAY the COAST || of DARK-ness LIES, <!-- 2+2: WAY COAST || DARK LIES [w/c/d/l: mixed] {opposition: coast ↔ lies—fixed edge versus receding dark} --> \
> Bor-der-ing on LIGHT. || When LO, the THRONE <!-- 2+2: LIGHT || LO THRONE [l/t: mixed] {opposition: light ↔ throne—order emerges from border} --> \
> Of CHAOS, CHIEF || of the CHURN-ing DEEP, <!-- 2+2: CHAOS CHIEF || CHURN DEEP [ch: aa|ax] {causation: Chaos as chief of churning} --> <!-- Pronunciation: Chaos = KAY-os, chief = CHEEF; ch-alliteration --> \
> His DARK pa-VI-lion || WIDE on the WASTE, <!-- 2+2: DARK pa-VI-lion || WIDE WASTE [w: ax|ax] {privation: pavilion wide on waste—spread on emptiness} --> \
> And NIGHT, enthroned, || in SABLE dressed, <!-- 2+2: NIGHT en-THRONED || SA-BLE DRESSED [n/s: mixed] {privation: Night dressed in sable—defined by dark garment} --> \
> El-dest of ALL, || his AN-cient QUEEN, <!-- 2+2: EL-dest ALL || AN-cient QUEEN [a: ax|ax] {privation: eldest ↔ ancient—age defined by age} --> <!-- FAIL: all and ancient share mood of vastness; retained for Night's epithet "eldest of things" --> \
> By THEM stood OR-cus || and HA-des, DREAD-ful, <!-- 2+2: THEM OR-cus || HA-des DREAD [t/h/d: mixed] {opposition: Orcus ↔ Hades—underworld twins in tension} --> \
> And the DREAD-ED NAME || of DE-mo-GOR-gon, <!-- 2+2: DREAD NAME || DE-mo-GOR-gon [d: aa|ax] {causation: name as product of dread} --> \
> RU-mor and CHANCE, || TUM-ult and TORN Confusion, <!-- 2+2: RU-mor CHANCE || TUM-ult TORN [r/ch/t: mixed] {like-on-like: abstractions embroiled} --> <!-- Guardrail: Confusion unalliterated; retained as named figure in catalog --> \
> And DIS-cord, MOUTH-MAD, || with her MANY MOUTHS. <!-- 2+2: DIS-cord MOUTH-MAD || MANY MOUTHS [m: aa|ax] {like-on-like: mouths enact their own madness} --> \
> </div>

## Claude-4.8-opus {#claude-4-8-opus-v3}

2026-06-11:

> <!-- FORM: 2 lifts per half-line (4/line), caesura " || ". Stave preference aa|ax; ax|ax frequent; head-stave (first lift of b-verse) always ties ≥1 a-verse lift. Cluster rule ADOPTED: st-/sp-/sc- bind only themselves; sh-, th-, ch- independent onsets; sw-/cr-/br-/etc. alliterate on first consonant. All vowels alliterate (license exploited for Orcus/awful, eldest/unlit). Pronunciation commitments: Demogorgon = de-mo-GOR-gon (g-onset, free lift); Arimaspians = ar-i-MAS-pi-ans (free lift, name does not bear the stave); Confusion = con-FU-sion (f-onset; sits in the unstaved catalog); Chaos = KAY-os (k); Orcus = OR-cus (vowel); Hades = HAY-deez (h, free lift). Deliberate deviations (all guardrailed on their lines): L6 medium-catalog {none}; L8 five-lift verb-catalog, half-2 unstaved; L12 final lift alliterates (Milton's "dwell"); L20 name-catalog {none}. -->
>
> <!-- ARGUMENT ARC: simile of the robbed griffin (1-4) → the fiend's crossing-catalog, terrain/body/verb (5-8) → the uproar, born blind, assaulting the ear (9-11) → his undaunted resolve and the border-question (12-14) → the court of Chaos: Chaos & Night enthroned, then the named powers in Milton's order down to Discord's thousand throats (15-22). -->
>
> <!-- SCANSION KEY: stressed lifts in CAPS in the per-line comments. Stave notation single-pipe (aa|ax) inside comments only; spaced " || " is the verse caesura. Tension vocabulary: opposition | privation | causation | like-on-like | inversion | violence. "—" in the stave slot marks a deliberately unstaved fidelity-catalog line. -->
>
> <!-- COMMENTARY:
>   FIDELITY MAP. Simile: griffin/wilderness (1, "gaunt … wind"); winged course over hill & marshy dale (2, "hills … wet fen"); Arimaspians named & present (3, free lift); "by stealth had stolen the guarded gold" (3-4, "hoarders by stealth / who had stolen its gold"—"guarded" implied by the griffin's ownership); "so eagerly the fiend" (4, "so strains the fiend," the st-stave bridging the theft of the vehicle to the strain of the tenor). Crossing: terrain "bog or steep" (5) and the four-fold medium "strait, rough, dense, or rare" preserved verbatim (6) as an honest unstaved catalog. Body-catalog "head, hands, wings, or feet" COMPRESSED to hand+heel (7); head & wings are carried by "winged" (2) and "flies" (8), and the 1.3x cap + no-padding rule argue against a dead catalog line—documented amplification-in-reverse. Verb catalog at FULL strength, all five (8: swims/sinks/wades/creeps/flies), as a deliberate 5-lift line. Uproar: "universal" → "a whole world of sound" (9, amplification serving the wild ↔ world stave); "stunning sounds and voices all confused" → "babble confounded" (10); "born through the hollow dark" (10, "born through the black"); "assaults his ear with loudest vehemence" → "batters his ear with the brunt of its rage" (11). Resolve: "Thither he plies, undaunted to meet whatever power or spirit … might dwell" (12-13); "nethermost abyss" → "the pit" (13); "nearest coast of darkness, bordering on light" → "where murk meets the morning, and parts" (14). Court, all figures in Milton's order: Chaos throned, dark pavilion → "cloak on the deep" (15, "spread wide on the wasteful deep" compressed); sable-vested Night, eldest of things (16); consort of his reign (17); Orcus, Hades (18); dreaded name of Demogorgon (19); Rumor, Chance, Tumult, Confusion, all embroiled (20-21); Discord with a thousand various mouths (22, "thousand throats … throngs the torn air," "various" → the throng/multitude).
>   KENNING LEDGER. "gaunt griffin" (1)—privation: the epithet ties the hunter to the hunger/loss the theft inflicts, so the chase is motivated, not decorative. "unlit Night" (16)—privation: Night defined by the light she has not. "the dumb-striking name" (19)—causation: presents the name as an agent that manufactures silence in the hearer (Milton's "dreaded name"). "brazen Discord" (21)—the only pure mood-epithet retained; justified as the catalog's closing flourish, one mood-word, on the unstaved final lift.
>   VERIFICATION. "gaunt griffin" → L1 ✓. "gives chase down the wind" → L1 ✓. "by strait or by rough, by the dense or the rare" → L6 ✓. "wades, creeps, or flies" → L8 ✓. "a whole world of sound" → L9 ✓. "unlit Night" → L16 ✓. "the dumb-striking name" / "dark Demogorgon" → L19 ✓. "brazen Discord" → L21 ✓. "thousand throats || throngs the torn air" → L22 ✓. All ledger entries and FORM examples present verbatim. -->
>
> <div class="poem">
> As when the gaunt griffin || gives chase down the wind, <!-- 2+2: GAUNT GRIFfin || GIVES WIND [g: aa|ax] {causation: gaunt ↔ gives} --> \
> winged over the hills, || the wet fen below, <!-- 2+2: WINGED HILLS || WET FEN [w: ax|ax] {opposition: winged ↔ wet} --> \
> to harry the Arimaspians, || hoarders by stealth <!-- 2+2: HARRY ar-i-MAS-pians || HOARDERS STEALTH [h: ax|ax] {violence: harry ↔ hoarders}; name = free lift --> \
> who had stolen its gold. || So strains the fiend, <!-- 2+2: STOLEN GOLD || STRAINS FIEND [st: ax|ax] {causation: stolen ↔ strains, vehicle→tenor} --> \
> over bog, over steep, || he beats out a road, <!-- 2+2: BOG STEEP || BEATS ROAD [b: ax|ax] {violence: bog ↔ beats} --> \
> by strait or by rough, || by the dense or the rare, <!-- 2+2: STRAIT ROUGH || DENSE RARE [—: xx|xx] {none: medium-catalog, retained verbatim for fidelity, graceful degradation} --> \

> <div class="poem">
> A griffin gives || gold-thieves chase, <!-- 2+2: GRIFF-in GIVES || GOLD-thieves CHASE [g: aa|ax] {violence: gives ↔ gold-thieves} --> \
> wings whip || wild marsh-dales, <!-- 2+2: WINGS WHIP || WILD MARSH-dales [w: aa|ax] {violence: whip ↔ wild marsh-dales} --> \
> hills he hammers || hunting Arimaspians, <!-- 2+2: HILLS HAM-mers || HUNT-ing A-ri-MAS-pi-ans [h: aa|ax] {violence: hammers ↔ hills} --> \
> guarded gold || they grabbed by stealth; <!-- 2+2: GUARD-ed GOLD || GRABBED STEALTH [g: aa|ax] {violence: grabbed ↔ guarded gold} --> \
> So fiend forces || fen-steep passage, <!-- 2+2: FIEND FORC-es || FEN-STEEP PAS-sage [f: aa|ax] {violence: forces ↔ fen-steep} --> \
> close-ways clutch him || coarse-ways cut, <!-- 2+2: CLOSE-ways CLUTCH || COARSE-ways CUT [c: aa|aa] {inversion: ways ↔ clutch/cut} --> \
> thin-ways thin him || thick-ways throttle, <!-- 2+2: THIN-ways THIN || THICK-ways THROT-tle [th: aa|aa] {like-on-like: thin ↔ thin; violence: thick-ways ↔ throttle} --> \
> head and hands || hammer hindrance, <!-- 2+2: HEAD HANDS || HAM-mer HIN-drance [h: aa|aa] {violence: hammer ↔ hindrance} --> \
> wings and feet || force formless way, <!-- 2+2: WINGS FEET || FORCE FORM-less-way [f: xa|aa] {causation: force ↔ formless way} --> \
> swims, sinks, wades || where swamps swallow, <!-- 3+2: SWIMS SINKS WADES || SWAMPS SWAL-low [s: aaa|aa] {inversion: swamps ↔ swallow; guardrail: extra first-half lift preserves the five-verb catalog} --> \
> crawls through clefts || then clears in flight. <!-- 2+2: CRAWLS CLEFTS || CLEARS FLIGHT [c: aa|ax] {violence: crawls ↔ clefts; clears ↔ clefts} --> \
>
> All-world uproar || eats at his ear, <!-- 2+2: ALL-world UP-roar || EATS EAR [vowel: aa|aa] {violence: eats ↔ ear} --> \
> deafening din || drowns dull sense, <!-- 2+2: DEAF-en-ing DIN || DROWNS DULL-sense [d: aa|aa] {violence: drowns ↔ dull sense} --> \
> voices vex voices || voice against voice, <!-- 2+2: VOIC-es VEX || VOICE VOICE [v: aa|aa] {like-on-like: voices ↔ voices} --> \
> hollow dark || hurls hard force. <!-- 2+2: HOL-low DARK || HURLS HARD-force [h: ax|aa] {violence: hurls ↔ hard force} --> \
>
> toward that thunder || he thrusts, unshaken, <!-- 2+2: THAT THUN-der || THRUSTS un-SHA-ken [th: aa|ax] {opposition: thrusts ↔ thunder} --> \
> to meet what might || or murk-born spirit <!-- 2+2: MEET MIGHT || MURK-born SPIR-it [m: aa|ax] {opposition: meet ↔ might} --> \
> in nether noise || might nest abyss-deep, <!-- 2+2: NETH-er NOISE || NEST A-BYSS-deep [n: aa|ax] {inversion: noise ↔ nest} --> \
> asks which dark coast || draws near dawn. <!-- 2+2: ASKS DARK-coast || DRAWS DAWN [d: xa|aa] {opposition: dark coast ↔ dawn} --> \
>
> Then Chaos claims || the crown-seat's rule; <!-- 2+2: KAY-os CLAIMS || CROWN-seat RULE [k: aa|ax] {opposition: Chaos ↔ rule} --> \
> black pavilion || blankets broad waste, <!-- 2+2: BLACK pa-VIL-ion || BLANK-ets BROAD-waste [b: ax|aa] {privation: black ↔ blankets} --> \
> wide, it swallows || the wasteful deep. <!-- 2+2: WIDE SWAL-lows || WASTE-ful DEEP [w: aa|ax] {violence: swallows ↔ wasteful deep} --> \
> Black-bride Night || sits bound beside him, <!-- 2+2: BLACK-bride NIGHT || BOUND be-SIDE [b: ax|aa] {inversion: bound ↔ bride} --> \
> oldest, she outlasts || all other things; <!-- 2+2: OLD-est OUT-lasts || ALL O-ther-things [vowel: aa|aa] {violence: outlasts ↔ other things} --> \
> Orcus; Hades || held hell-watch there, <!-- 2+2: OR-cus HAY-deez || HELD HELL-watch [h: xa|aa] {causation: held ↔ hell-watch} --> \
> Dread Demogorgon || gags grave-name. <!-- 2+2: DREAD GOR-gon || GAGS GRAVE-name [g: xa|aa] {violence: gags ↔ grave-name} --> \
> Rumor rattles || random Chance, <!-- 2+2: ROO-mor RAT-tles || RAN-dom CHANCE [r: aa|ax] {violence: rattles ↔ random Chance} --> \
> Tumult tangles || tight Confusion, <!-- 2+2: TUM-ult TAN-gles || TIGHT con-FU-sion [t: aa|ax] {violence: tangles ↔ tight Confusion} --> \

## Claude-5-Fable {#claude-5-fable-v3}

2026-06-11:

> <!-- PERISHED PARADISE—Paradise Lost II.943–967 (the griffin simile through the court of Chaos),
>      translated into modern alliterative verse on the Old English model. -->
>
> <!-- FORM
> 2 lifts per half-line (4 per line); caesura marked " || "; one deliberate exception: l.16 carries a fifth lift to keep Milton's "nethermost".
> Stave preference aa|ax (13 of 28 lines); remainder ax|ax or xa|ax; no line ends on an alliterating fourth lift; no line lacks a cross-caesura stave.
> Cluster rule ADOPTED: st-/sp-/sc- alliterate only with themselves ("by stealth had stolen the stare-watched gold", l.4; "spirit sprung", l.16); sh- and th- are independent of s- and t-; th- groups with thr- ("Chaos enthroned, thrust", l.19); sw-/sl-/sn- count as plain s- ("he swims, he sinks, he soars", l.9). Consequence: Milton's "stunning sounds" is unstaveable under this rule and was paraphrased away (l.12).
> Vowel license: all vowels mutually alliterate ("utter uproar, all order", l.11; "ask of it only the end", l.17; "Orcus and old", l.23).
> No end-rhyme (swept, cycle 3). No archaisms.
> -->
>
> <!-- ARGUMENT ARC
> ¶1 (1–5): the robbed-griffin simile, the theft doubled into the victim's epithet, pivot to the fiend.
> ¶2 (6–10): the crossing—terrain catalog, member catalog, verb catalog; toil wins passage.
> ¶3 (11–13): the uproar, made weather and assailant, strikes the ear.
> ¶4 (14–18): the resolve and the single question—darkness petitioned for news of its own end.
> ¶5 (19–28): the court of Chaos in Milton's order, closing on Discord's untuned tongues.
> -->
>
> <!-- SCANSION KEY
> Per-line audit: <!- n | 2+2: LIFT LIFT || LIFT LIFT [stave: pattern] {tension: x ↔ y} ->
> Lifts in CAPS (for polysyllables, only the stressed syllable is capitalized). Single-pipe pattern notation (aa|ax) is audit-only; " || " is the verse caesura.
> Tension vocabulary: opposition | privation | causation | like-on-like | inversion | violence.
> Pronunciations: Arimaspians = a-ri-MAS-pi-ans (lift falls on MAS; the name is never asked to carry a stave). Demogorgon = de-mo-GOR-gon (staves on g). "Pavilion" avoided: its lift (-VIL-) would stave on v; rendered "tent".
> -->
>
> <!-- COMMENTARY
> FIDELITY MAP. Wilderness→"the wild" (1); winged course→"winging his course" (2); hill/marshy dale→"wold and marsh" (2; dale folded into the pairing); Arimaspians (3); stealth/stolen (4, Milton's own figura etymologica kept on one st-stave); guarded→"stare-watched" (4; the griffin's vigilance made the lock); eagerly→"hungry…hurls" (5); bog/steep/strait/rough (6); dense/rare→"thick and thin" (7); head/hands/wings/feet→"head…hands…heel…wing" (8); the five verbs→swims, sinks, soars (=flies), creeps (9), wades (10); "at length"→"Then" (11); universal uproar wild→"utter uproar, all order drowned" (11); stunning sounds and voices confused→"a brawl of voices bearing no sense" (12; the cluster rule forbids staving "stunning sounds"); borne through the hollow dark/assaults his ear/loudest vehemence→"down the hollow dark hails on his ear" (13; vehemence compressed into the storm-verb); thither he plies→"drives at the din's heart" (14); whatever power or spirit→"whatever might…power or spirit" (15–16); nethermost abyss→"the nethermost floor" (16); dwell in that noise→"is master of the howl" (15); of whom to ask which way the nearest coast of darkness lies→"to ask of it only the end of the dark" (17; "nearest" absorbed); bordering on light→"where the borders of blackness break upon light" (18; the coast survives as surf in the verb); when lo, the throne of Chaos→"There: Chaos enthroned" (19); dark pavilion spread wide on the wasteful deep→"his tent of blackness blanketing the waste" (20); with him enthroned sat→"sat by him" (21; the throne having just been named); sable-vested→"black-gowned" (22); eldest of things→"known to no elder" (21); consort of his reign→"the bride and bond of his reign" (22); Orcus, Hades (23); dreaded name of Demogorgon→"graver than gods, Demogorgon's name" (24); Rumor next, and Chance→"Rumor and Chance ran at their heels" (25); Tumult and Confusion, all embroiled→"twisted together" (26); Discord with a thousand various mouths→"her doors of din a thousand, no tongue tuned to the tongue beside it" (27–28). Named figures appear in Milton's order. 28 lines against 23 source lines (cap 29).
> AMPLIFICATIONS (each serving a tension): "gold-robbed" (1) doubles the theft into the victim's epithet; "hungry…hurls" (5) binds the fiend's appetite to the griffin's; "only" (17) sharpens the embassy to a single treacherous question; "master" (15) promotes Milton's "dwell" to the rule his context implies; "old" Hades (23) externalizes "eldest of things" onto the whole court; "graver than gods" (24) makes the name's dread explicit; "bond" (22) reads consort as fetter; ll.27–28 unfold "various" into tongues refusing unison, so the poem ends by enacting Discord.
> KENNING LEDGER (verbatim, with line): "gold-robbed" (1)—privation: the guardian named by what was taken. "stare-watched gold" (4)—causation: the griffin's gaze as the lock on the hoard. "the din's heart" (14)—inversion: noise granted anatomy and a center to strike at. "tent of blackness" (20)—causation: darkness woven into architecture. "black-gowned" (22)—opposition: the wedding garment in grave-color. "bride and bond" (22)—causation: the consort as the fetter of the reign. "her doors of din" (27)—causation: mouths as gates manufacturing noise.
> VERIFICATION: ledger ↔ text checked both ways; every quoted phrase above occurs verbatim at the cited line, and every FORM example quotes ll.4, 9, 11, 16, 17, 19, 23 verbatim.
> -->
>
> <div class="poem">
> As the griffin, gold-robbed, || goes through the wild, <!-- 1 | 2+2: GRIF-fin GOLD-robbed || GOES WILD [g: aa|ax] {privation: griffin ↔ gold—the guardian named by what was taken} --> \
> winging his course || over wold and marsh, <!-- 2 | 2+2: WING-ing COURSE || WOLD MARSH [w: ax|ax] {opposition: wing ↔ wold—the air-course yoked to the ground it spurns} --> \
> to press the Arimaspians, || plunderers sly, <!-- 3 | 2+2: PRESS -MAS- || PLUN-derers SLY [p: ax|ax] {violence: press ↔ plunderers—pursuit's force bound to the crime that caused it} --> \
> who by stealth had stolen || the stare-watched gold: <!-- 4 | 2+2: STEALTH STO-len || STARE-watched GOLD [st: aa|ax] {like-on-like: stealth ↔ stolen—Milton's theft named twice, method fused to act} --> \
> so hungry the fiend || hurls himself on, <!-- 5 | 2+2: HUN-gry FIEND || HURLS ON [h: ax|ax] {inversion: hurls himself—the fiend at once whip and beast} --> \
> over bog, up steep, || through strait and rough; <!-- 6 | 2+2: BOG STEEP || STRAIT ROUGH [st: xa|ax] {opposition: steep ↔ strait—the rise against the squeeze; verbless terrain catalog, verb lands in 7} --> \
> through thick and thin || he thrusts ahead <!-- 7 | 2+2: THICK THIN || THRUSTS a-HEAD [th: aa|ax] {opposition: thick ↔ thin, plus violence: thrusts through both media} --> \
> with head, with hands, || with heel and wing: <!-- 8 | 2+2: HEAD HANDS || HEEL WING [h: aa|ax] {like-on-like: limb ↔ limb—every member alike pressed into passage; catalog quota spent here} --> \
> he swims, he sinks, || he soars, he creeps, <!-- 9 | 2+2: SWIMS SINKS || SOARS CREEPS [s: aa|ax] {opposition: swims ↔ sinks—buoyancy against drowning; every stave a finite verb} --> \
> he wades and wins || his way through the murk. <!-- 10 | 2+2: WADES WINS || WAY MURK [w: aa|ax] {causation: wading wins the way—passage manufactured by toil} --> \
> Then an utter uproar, || all order drowned, <!-- 11 | 2+2: UT-ter UP-roar || OR-der DROWNED [vowel: aa|ax] {opposition: uproar ↔ order—noise as order's flood; verb sits on the unstaved fourth lift, retained} --> \
> a brawl of voices || bearing no sense, <!-- 12 | 2+2: BRAWL VOIC-es || BEAR-ing SENSE [b: ax|ax] {privation: brawl ↔ bearing no sense—sound stripped of message} --> \
> down the hollow dark || hails on his ear. <!-- 13 | 2+2: HOL-low DARK || HAILS EAR [h: ax|ax] {inversion+violence: the dark made weather, the ear its ground} --> \
> Undaunted he drives || at the din's heart <!-- 14 | 2+2: un-DAUNT-ed DRIVES || DIN'S HEART [d: aa|ax] {opposition: undaunted ↔ din—fearlessness yoked to the noise meant to daunt it} --> \
> to meet whatever might || is master of the howl, <!-- 15 | 2+2: MEET MIGHT || MAS-ter HOWL [m: aa|ax] {inversion: a howl owning a master; opposition: the challenger yoked to the power he braves} --> \
> power or spirit sprung || from the nethermost floor, <!-- 16 | 2+3: POW-er SPIR-it || SPRUNG NETH-er-most FLOOR [sp: xa|ax] {inversion: spirit ↔ sprung from the floor—rank rooted at the bottom} fifth lift kept for Milton's "nethermost" --> \
> to ask of it only || the end of the dark, <!-- 17 | 2+2: ASK ON-ly || END DARK [vowel: aa|ax] {privation: ask ↔ end—darkness petitioned for news of its own death} --> \
> where the borders of blackness || break upon light. <!-- 18 | 2+2: BOR-ders BLACK-ness || BREAK LIGHT [b: aa|ax] {violence: blackness breaks upon light—the coast as surf shattering on day} --> \
> There: Chaos enthroned, || thrust from the gloom, <!-- 19 | 2+2: CHA-os en-THRONED || THRUST GLOOM [th: xa|ax] {inversion: majesty thrust at the traveler—the court ambushes its guest} --> \
> his tent of blackness || blanketing the waste; <!-- 20 | 2+2: TENT BLACK-ness || BLANK-eting WASTE [b: xa|ax] {opposition: blackness ↔ waste—shelter spread over the shelterless} --> \
> and Night sat by him, || known to no elder, <!-- 21 | 2+2: NIGHT SAT || KNOWN EL-der [n: ax|ax] {privation: Night ↔ no elder—primacy stated as the absence of any before her} --> \
> black-gowned, the bride || and bond of his reign; <!-- 22 | 2+2: BLACK-gowned BRIDE || BOND REIGN [b: aa|ax] {opposition: black ↔ bride—the wedding garment in grave-color; causation: consort as bond} verbless appositive line, mirroring Milton's apposition --> \
> by them stood Orcus || and old Hades, <!-- 23 | 2+2: STOOD OR-cus || OLD HA-des [vowel: xa|ax] {like-on-like: Orcus ↔ old—eld attending eld; weakest stave in the poem, retained for Milton's name-order} --> \
> and, graver than gods, || Demogorgon's name; <!-- 24 | 2+2: GRAV-er GODS || -GOR-gon NAME [g: aa|ax] {inversion: a name outranking gods—the word seated among persons; verbless because its courtier is bodiless} --> \
> Rumor and Chance || ran at their heels, <!-- 25 | 2+2: RU-mor CHANCE || RAN HEELS [r: ax|ax] {inversion: Rumor given legs—hearsay made a body that runs} --> \
> Tumult and Confusion || twisted together, <!-- 26 | 2+2: TU-mult -FU-sion || TWIST-ed -GETH-er [t: ax|ax] {like-on-like: disorder twisted with disorder—"embroiled" enacted} --> \
> and Discord, her doors || of din a thousand, <!-- 27 | 2+2: DIS-cord DOORS || DIN THOU-sand [d: aa|ax] {causation: Discord ↔ doors of din—strife as a thousand-gated house of noise; verbless: her speech rendered as architecture} --> \
> no tongue tuned || to the tongue beside it. <!-- 28 | 2+2: TONGUE TUNED || TONGUE be-SIDE [t: aa|ax] {like-on-like: tongue ↔ tongue—identical organs refusing unison; "tuned" the verb-derived stave} --> \
> </div>

# v4

Changes from v3, all motivated by the v3 test round: (1) quarantine globalized to ALL quoted verse anywhere in the prompt (v3's violence-definition example "wings whip the waste" was imported by 3 of 6 models); violence illustration reworded to be unmappable onto the passage; (2) violence anti-stamp: confabulation counter-example "{violence: outlasts ↔ other things}" (GPT-5.5) + relation-diversity check in Stage 3 (violence tagged >half of GPT-5.5's lines); (3) verse text in sentence case, CAPS confined to audit comments (DeepSeek-v4 & Kimi leaked scansion CAPS into the poem); (4) staved final lift (aa|aa) is a per-instance guardrailed deviation (GPT-5.5 ran ~10 unflagged); (5) cluster rule disambiguated: only st-/sp-/sc- are fixed clusters, str-/spr-/scr- group with them, sw-/sl-/sn-/sm- count as plain s- (Fable and Claude-4.8 split on sw- in v3). -->

Translate the passage of John Milton's _Paradise Lost_ given at the end of this prompt into modern English alliterative verse on the Old English model. The project is dubbed _Perished Paradise_: a glimpse of the alliterative ur-epic Milton might have written had _Genesis B_, not Virgil, been his formal master.

**Global quarantine.** Every line of verse quoted anywhere in this prompt—exemplars, counter-exemplars, definitions, worked examples—is quarantined: it demonstrates a standard or a failure, and is not material. Reusing quarantined phrasing, epithets, or close variants in your translation FAILS the line. Invent your own tensions.

## Form

- 4 stressed syllables per line, divided by a marked caesura (` || `). Every verse line contains exactly one `||`.
- Alliteration binds stressed syllables across the caesura. Prefer the classical `aa|ax` pattern: both lifts of the first half-line and the first lift of the second half-line alliterate; the final lift does not. `ax|ax` is acceptable; a line whose halves share no stave is a defect. **A staved final lift (`aa|aa`, `ax|aa`) is a deviation: each instance requires an inline guardrail on its own line, not a blanket license in FORM.**
- **Notation:** the single-pipe form (`aa|ax`) is reserved for pattern notation inside audit comments; the spaced ` || ` is the caesura mark in verse text. Never interchange them. **Verse text is written in normal sentence case; CAPS appear only inside audit comments, never in the poem itself.**
- Alliteration is by the initial sound of the stressed syllable. All vowels alliterate with one another (exploit this for classical names: Orcus / awe / ash).
- **Cluster rule (recommended; declare your choice in the FORM block).** Only st-, sp-, and sc-/sk- are fixed clusters, alliterating solely with themselves (str-, spr-, scr- group with them); all other s-clusters (sw-, sl-, sn-, sm-) count as plain s-. sh- and th- are independent onsets, distinct from s- and t-. This is authentic Old English practice and costs little. If you decline it, say so in FORM rather than silently mixing onsets.
- No end-rhyme. If rhyme creeps in incidentally, remove it: rhyme is the gravity well this form must escape.
- Modern English vocabulary throughout. No Old English words (no _wyrd_, _nið_, _sæ-_), no faux-archaism ("'twas," "doth"; "o'er" tolerated sparingly). The intended reader knows neither Old English nor Milton; the poem must stand alone and give pleasure unassisted.

## Fidelity

This is translation, not imitation. Every substantive element of Milton's passage must survive recognizably: similes with their referents intact, verb catalogs at full strength, named figures present and in order, the narrative sequence unbroken. Rearrange syntax and lineation freely; compress connective tissue; do not drop content or invent episodes.

**Length cap.** Target at most 1.3× the source's line count. Amplification is permitted only when it serves a tension (eg. doubling the theft into the victim's epithet), and every amplification must be documented in the fidelity map. Padding to feed the stave is a fidelity failure, not a formal success.

## The cardinal rule: alliteration must argue

In Old English verse, alliteration is syntax, not ornament: the stave yokes the two most important words of the line and asserts a relation between them. Your alliterating words must stand in semantic tension—they must argue, not agree. The acceptable relations:

1. **Opposition.** The stave binds antagonists, so the sound enacts the border between them.
2. **Privation.** An epithet defined by lack; the stave ties the subject to the very thing it is denied.
3. **Causation / craft.** Agent bound to product; an abstraction presented as deliberate manufacture.
4. **Violence of like upon like.** Repetition enacting the self-destruction it describes.
5. **Agency inversion.** An abstraction made the actor, a body or person made the patient.
6. **Violence.** Asymmetric force: a literal force-verb on the stave, its concrete victim on the stave beside it (the shape of ["frost fells the forest"]{.poem}—assailant, blow, and broken thing all bound by one sound). Distinct from like-on-like in that the combatants differ. **This is the easiest relation to fake:** an ordinary transitive verb is not violence. "{violence: outlasts ↔ other things}" is grammar wearing a war-mask; if the verb would not bruise, the tag is confabulated.

**Forbidden: alliterating synonyms or mood-twins.** ["Grim griffin glides through gloomy glens"]{.poem} contains four words that all mean "dark and scary"; the line asserts nothing and FAILS no matter how many staves it carries. One mood-word per stave, maximum. A line with two arguing staves beats a line with five agreeing ones.

**Forbidden: grammar masquerading as tension.** A claimed relation must survive this check: would it be equally true of any two words in an ordinary sentence? "{causation: griffin ↔ grips—the act defines the hunter}" fails the check, because every agent is "defined by" its verb; that is grammar, not tension. So is "perception bound to its object" for any verb of seeing, and so is any transitive verb promoted to {violence}. If the only honest tag is `{none}`, write `{none}` and rewrite the line; a confabulated tag is worse than an honest defect, because it corrupts the audit trail that justifies the whole apparatus.

**Verbs must carry staves.** In each line, at least one alliterating position should be a finite verb or verb-derived word, and that verb should do something *to* another alliterating word. Catalogs of compound nouns (["_X_-_Y_ noun || _Z_-_W_ noun"]{.poem}) are permitted at most once per 8 lines, reserved for formal set-pieces such as the court of Chaos—and even there, the compounds must each pass the tension test.

## Exemplar lines

The exemplars below are drawn from prior translations of this very passage and fall under the global quarantine above. Some also predate the strict stress-count; imitate their stave-logic, not their meter.

Study these before drafting. Each is annotated with the tension its stave asserts; this annotation format is the one you will apply to your own lines.

<div class="poem">
> to ask where mirk-marches meet morning, where dark \
> gives ground to the gleam, at the grey verge of light
</div>

— Opposition. The m-stave and g-stave each bind darkness to the daylight it borders; "gives ground" puts the boundary's own action on the stave—dark literally retreats. The alliteration *is* the border being described.

<div class="poem">
> Sound against sound, voice into voice broken
</div>

— Violence of like upon like. The repetition performs the self-destruction it reports; "broken" lands as the unalliterated final lift, the one word the pattern cannot absorb.

<div class="poem">
> Sable-swathed Night, star-shorn, first-born
</div>

— Privation. The s-stave ties Night to the stars she lacks; she is defined by deprivation, and the sound will not let her escape it.

<div class="poem">
> Old as oblivion, his anguished queen
</div>

— Vowel-stave binding age to erasure: to be as old as oblivion is to be coeval with forgetting. "Anguished" gives the personification interiority rather than costume.

<div class="poem">
> By her the hush held and the heart grew cold
</div>

— Agency inversion. The h-stave makes silence the actor and the heart the patient; an absence is granted grip.

<div class="poem">
> So storms the fiend. The fear-smith fares
</div>

— Causation/craft: "fear-smith" presents woe as deliberate manufacture, and the verbs "storms"/"fares" carry their staves rather than leaving the work to adjectives.

Counter-exemplar, for calibration: ["Doom-depths dire now drive dread-sounds deep"]{.poem}—6 staves, zero claims; every word restates "scary"; the verb is inert. FAIL.

## Procedure

Work in one pass through three internal stages. Show all three.

**Stage 1—Draft A (sense-first).** Translate the passage into the form above, prioritizing syntax, clarity, and narrative momentum. Alliterate where it comes naturally; never wrench word order for a stave. This draft establishes what the lines must *say*.

**Stage 2—Draft B (sound-first).** Re-translate, now maximizing sound-density: kennings, compounds, heavy staves, appositive variation. Push past good taste—most of this draft will be cut. Draft B exists only to generate candidate sound-effects for the hybrid; it is ore, not metal.

**Stage 3—Audit and hybrid.** Apply the following test to every line of both drafts, then compose the final hybrid keeping Draft A's syntax and momentum and importing from Draft B only those effects that pass:

For each line, state:

#. which words carry the staves;
#. what claim the binding makes—which relation of opposition, privation, causation, like-on-like, inversion, or violence the sound asserts;
#. PASS or FAIL. If the honest answer to (2) is "none—the words merely share a mood or restate each other," the line FAILS regardless of how many alliterations it contains, and must be rewritten so that at least one stave falls on a verb or on a pair of words in tension.

**Calibration check:** your audit of Draft B should FAIL at least a third of its lines. Draft B was written past good taste; if your audit passes nearly everything, your audit is too lenient, not your draft too good.

**Relation-diversity check:** if any single relation tags more than half your lines, re-audit those tags before delivery. The commonest confabulation is promoting ordinary transitivity to {violence}; the second commonest is stretching {causation} over plain agent-verb grammar. A diverse tag profile is not required, but a monoculture is presumptive evidence of stamping.

Re-audit the hybrid and revise until every line passes, or until you can state in one clause why a FAIL is deliberately retained. **Anti-loop rule:** every audit cycle must change the text or terminate with that justification; never emit an unchanged version and call it improved. Three cycles should suffice; endless polishing is itself a failure mode.

**Graceful degradation.** Full compliance is not always possible—Milton's five-verb catalog cannot stave completely, and some proper names resist every pattern. When constraints conflict, deliver the poem with honest FAIL tags and inline guardrails rather than refusing the task or fabricating compliance. An honestly documented deviation is conformant; a confabulated tag is not. The audit trail, not perfection, is the deliverable.

## Deliverable

Format the final poem for direct pasting into a Gwern.net source file, following its poetry conventions exactly:

1. **Poem block.** Wrap the poem in `<div class="poem">`{.HTML}. Every verse line ends with ` \`{.Markdown}, and the backslash must be the *last character on the line*—all HTML comments therefore go *before* the backslash, never after. Mark the caesura with ` || `{.Markdown} (space, double-pipe, space). Verse text in normal sentence case—CAPS belong only in the audit comments. Separate verse paragraphs with a single blank line.
2. **Header comment blocks**, placed immediately before the poem block, in this order:

   - `<!-- FORM ... -->`{.HTML}—the metrical scheme actually achieved: stress counts per half-line, stave-pattern preference (`aa|ax`), a count of staved-final-lift deviations (each also guardrailed on its line), cluster-rule choice (adopted or declined), vowel-alliteration license for classical names, and every other deliberate deviation, each in one line.
   - `<!-- ARGUMENT ARC ... -->`{.HTML}—what each verse paragraph does in the narrative progression (eg. simile → crossing-catalog → uproar → resolve → court of Chaos).
   - `<!-- SCANSION KEY ... -->`{.HTML}—defines the per-line audit format below: stress-casing convention (stressed syllables in CAPS, audit comments only), stave notation (single-pipe `aa|ax` in audit comments, never the spaced verse caesura), tension-tag vocabulary (opposition | privation | causation | like-on-like | inversion | violence), and pronunciation commitments for any unstable words (classical names especially: eg. `Demogorgon = de-mo-GOR-gon`).
   - `<!-- COMMENTARY ... -->`{.HTML}—two parts. **Fidelity map:** where each Miltonic element went (simile sources, verb catalogs, order of named figures), the reasoning for any compression, and every amplification per the length cap. **Kenning ledger:** every compound and epithet retained in the final text, each justified in one clause by the tension it asserts; any kenning you cannot justify in one clause must be cut before delivery, not excused after it. The ledger covers your coinages, not Milton's own phrases carried over—padding the ledger with source-text is inflation, not compliance.
   - **Verification (delivery-blocking).** Every kenning-ledger entry and every illustrative example in FORM must quote a phrase present verbatim in the final poem, with its line number. Before delivery, check the correspondence both ways: a ledger entry with no matching line, or a FORM example quoting words the poem does not contain, is a delivery-blocking error, not a footnote.
3. **Per-line audit comments.** Each verse line carries a post-comment (before the backslash) compressing its Stage-3 audit into one line of mechanical notation—stressed lifts in CAPS, stave letter and pattern in brackets, tension tag in braces:

   ~~~{.HTML}
   <!-- 2+2: LIFT LIFT || LIFT lift [stave: pattern] {tension: word ↔ word} -->
   ~~~

   Worked example (the line is from the quarantined exemplars; it illustrates the comment format only):

   ~~~{.HTML}
   Sable-swathed Night, || star-shorn, first-born, <!-- 2+2: SA-ble NIGHT || STAR-SHORN BORN [s: aa|ax] {privation: Night ↔ stars} --> \
   ~~~

   The `{tension}` tag names which relation (opposition | privation | causation | like-on-like | inversion | violence) the stave asserts, and between which words. The unalliterated final lift claims nothing and needs no tag of its own. A line whose tag would honestly read `{none}` should not have survived Stage 3; if one is retained anyway (eg. for fidelity), say so in the same comment.
4. **Inline guardrails.** Deliberate rule violations—an extra lift, a staved final lift, a mood-word pair kept for fidelity, an unusual word choice—are documented in an HTML comment on the offending line itself, not summarized elsewhere.

## The passage

<!-- Deliberate choice: this is the modernized griffin passage used in every prior run (Gwern & GPT-4 2023), not Milton's 1674 orthography ("Gryfon ... moarie Dale ... universal hubbub"), retained for cross-run comparability. -->

<div class="poem">
"As when a griffin through the wilderness, \
With winged course over hill or marshy dale, \
Pursues the Arimaspians, who by stealth \
Had stolen the guarded gold, so eagerly the fiend \
O'er bog or steep, through strait, rough, dense, or rare, \
With head, hands, wings, or feet pursues his way, \
And swims or sinks, or wades, or creeps, or flies. \
At length, a universal uproar wild \
Of stunning sounds and voices all confused, \
Born through the hollow dark, assaults his ear \
With loudest vehemence. Thither he plies, \
Undaunted to meet whatever power or spirit \
Of the nethermost abyss might dwell in that noise, \
Of whom to ask which way the nearest coast of darkness lies, \
Bordering on light. When lo, the throne \
Of Chaos and his dark pavilion spread \
Wide on the wasteful deep; with him enthroned \
Sat sable-vested Night, eldest of things, \
The consort of his reign, and by them stood \
Orcus and Hades, and the dreaded name \
Of Demogorgon; Rumor next, and Chance, \
And Tumult and Confusion, all embroiled, \
And Discord with a thousand various mouths."
</div>

> born from the blackness || to batter his ears. <!-- 2+2: BORN BLACK-ness || BAT-ter EARS [b: aa|ax] {violence: batter ↔ ears} --> \
> Fearless he faces || the force of the din, <!-- 2+2: FEAR-less FAC-es || FORCE DIN [f: aa|ax] {opposition: fearless ↔ force} --> \
> the phantom or power || in the pitiless gulf, <!-- 2+2: PHAN-tom POW-er || PIT-i-less GULF [p: aa|ax] {privation: pitiless} --> \
> to ask where the edge || of the endless void <!-- 2+2: ASK EDGE || END-less VOID [vowels: aa|ax] {opposition: edge ↔ endless} --> \
> loses its lands || to the limits of day. <!-- 2+2: LOS-es LANDS || LIM-its DAY [l: aa|ax] {opposition: lands ↔ limits} --> \
>
> Then hurled on the heavings, || the high throne of Chaos, <!-- 2+2: HURLED HEAV-ings || HIGH CHA-os [h: aa|ax] {violence: hurled ↔ heavings} --> \
> a tent on the tide || of the tumbling deep; <!-- 2+2: TENT TIDE || TUM-bling DEEP [t: aa|ax] {opposition: tent ↔ tumbling} --> \
> ruling the ruin || in robes of black, <!-- 2+2: RUL-ing RU-in || ROBES BLACK [r: aa|ax] {opposition: ruling ↔ ruin} --> \
> sits Night the newborn-less, || the nurse of the void. <!-- 2+2: NIGHT NEW-born-less || NURSE VOID [n: aa|ax] {privation: newborn-less} --> \
> Orcus and eager, || ash-pale Hades, <!-- 2+2: OR-cus EA-ger || ASH-pale HA-des [vowels: aa|ax] {opposition: eager ↔ ash-pale} --> \
> the dreaded doom-speaker, || Demogorgon by name; <!-- 2+2: DREAD-ed DOOM-speak-er || DE-mo-gor-gon NAME [d: aa|ax] {causation: doom-speaker} --> \
> Rumor is running || with ruinous Chance, <!-- 2+2: RU-mor RUN-ning || RU-in-ous CHANCE [r: aa|ax] {inversion: ruinous ↔ Chance} --> \
> fighting the formless || Confusion and Tumult, <!-- 2+2: FIGHT-ing FORM-less || CON-FU-sion TU-mult [f: aa|ax] {violence: fighting ↔ formless} --> \
> and a thousand of throats || for the thundering Discord. <!-- 2+2: THOU-sand THROATS || THUN-der-ing DIS-cord [th: aa|ax] {violence: thundering ↔ Discord} --> \
> </div>

> Fidelity map: lines 1-4 render the griffin simile, the Arimaspians, stealth, and the stolen protected gold; lines 5-7 render the fiend's passage over bog, steep, strait, rough, dense, and rare conditions; lines 8-11 render head, hands, wings, feet, plus swims, sinks, wades, creeps, and flies; lines 12-15 render the universal wild uproar, stunning sound, confused voices, dark transmission, assault on hearing, loud force, and undaunted will; lines 16-18 render his movement toward whatever power or spirit may dwell in the noise of the abyss; lines 19-20 render the question about the nearest coast of darkness bordering light; lines 21-22 render Chaos, throne, dark pavilion, breadth, waste, and deep; lines 23-24 render black-clad Night as eldest and consort; lines 25-29 preserve the order Orcus, Hades, Demogorgon, Rumor, Chance, Tumult, Confusion, Discord, and the thousand mouths.

> Amplification: line 1 "gold-starved griffin" makes the theft wound the pursuer; line 13 "crushed all sense" makes stunning sound bodily; line 19 "pinched darkness" turns nearest-route seeking into a constriction of dark; line 29 "thousand-mouthed" compresses the thousand various mouths into Discord's epithet.
> Kenning ledger: line 1 "gold-starved griffin" ties the pursuer to the gold it lacks; line 3 "hoard-thieves" makes the Arimaspians legible as theft-agents; line 4 "fire-gold" presents the treasure as a dangerous lure; line 4 "fenced stores" renders guarded wealth as enclosure violated by theft; line 7 "over-thick" turns density into excess opposed by emptied thinness; line 14 "empty dark" makes the hollow dark an attacking absence; line 18 "bottomless black" renders the abyss as generative depth without floor; line 20 "nearest night-coast" compresses nearest coast of darkness into a boundary-kenning; line 22 "pavilion, black" carries the dark pavilion without Milton's wording; line 22 "ruin-depth" renders the wasteful deep as destructive depth; line 23 "black-clad" carries Night's dark vesture; line 25 "awe-named Demogorgon" makes dread the maker of the name; line 29 "thousand-mouthed" carries the thousand various mouths into Discord's body.
> Verification: every ledger phrase appears verbatim in the final poem at the listed line; no FORM example quotes absent text.
> -->

> <div class="poem">
> As a gold-starved griffin || grinds the waste, <!-- 2+2: GOLD-STARVED GRIFFIN || GRINDS WASTE [g: aa|ax] {privation: griffin ↔ gold} --> \
> with wings that wound || wetland and ridge, <!-- 2+2: WINGS WOUND || WETLAND RIDGE [w: aa|ax] {violence: wound ↔ wetland} --> \
> hunts Arimaspians, || hoard-thieves by stealth, <!-- 2+2: HUNTS ARIMASPIANS || HOARD-THIEVES STEALTH [h: ax|ax] {violence: hunts ↔ hoard-thieves} --> \
> who filched fire-gold || from fenced stores, <!-- 2+2: FILCHED FIRE-GOLD || FENCED STORES [f: aa|ax] {opposition: filched ↔ fenced} --> \
> so the fiend forces || fen and cliff, <!-- 2+2: FIEND FORCES || FEN CLIFF [f: aa|ax] {opposition: fiend ↔ fen} --> \
> where narrows nip || nerve on rough stone, <!-- 2+2: NARROWS NIP || NERVE STONE [n: aa|ax] {violence: nip ↔ nerve} --> \
> where air, over-thick, || is emptied thin, <!-- 2+2: AIR OVER-THICK || EMPTIED THIN [vowel: aa|ax] {opposition: over-thick ↔ emptied} --> \
> head and hands || hammer a road, <!-- 2+2: HEAD HANDS || HAMMER ROAD [h: aa|ax] {causation: hands ↔ hammer} --> \
> feet and wings || wrench passage, <!-- 2+2: FEET WINGS || WRENCH PASSAGE [w: ax|ax] {causation: wings ↔ wrench} --> \
> swims, then sinks; || the surge takes him, <!-- 2+2: SWIMS SINKS || SURGE TAKES [s: aa|ax] {opposition: swims ↔ sinks} --> \
> wading, crawling, || he wings into air. <!-- 2+2: WADING CRAWLING || WINGS AIR [w: ax|ax] {opposition: wading ↔ wings} --> \
>
> Then all air || erupted in roar, <!-- 2+2: ALL AIR || ERUPTED ROAR [vowel: aa|ax] {inversion: air ↔ erupted} --> \
> crash and cries || crushed all sense, <!-- 2+2: CRASH CRIES || CRUSHED SENSE [k: aa|ax] {like-on-like: crash ↔ cries} --> \
> empty dark aimed || at his hearing, <!-- 2+2: EMPTY DARK || AIMED HEARING [vowel: ax|ax] {inversion: empty ↔ aimed} --> \
> fierce force failed || before his will; <!-- 2+2: FIERCE FORCE || FAILED WILL [f: aa|ax] {opposition: force ↔ failed} --> \
> he bent to the brawl || to brave whatever <!-- 2+2: BENT BRAWL || BRAVE WHATEVER [b: aa|ax] {opposition: brave ↔ brawl} --> \
> power or spirit, || penned in noise <!-- 2+2: POWER SPIRIT || PENNED NOISE [p: ax|ax] {privation: power ↔ penned} --> \
> from bottomless black, || still breeding uproar, <!-- 2+2: BOTTOMLESS BLACK || BREEDING UPROAR [b: aa|ax] {causation: black ↔ breeding} --> \
> what path, he asked, || pinched darkness <!-- 2+2: PATH ASKED || PINCHED DARKNESS [p: ax|ax] {inversion: path ↔ pinched} --> \
> to nearest night-coast || narrowed by light. <!-- 2+2: NEAREST NIGHT-COAST || NARROWED LIGHT [n: aa|ax] {privation: night-coast ↔ narrowed} --> \
>
> Then Chaos claimed || his cracked throne, <!-- 2+2: CHAOS CLAIMED || CRACKED THRONE [k: aa|ax] {causation: Chaos ↔ cracked} --> \
> pavilion, black, || pitched across ruin-depth; <!-- 2+2: PAVILION BLACK || PITCHED RUIN-DEPTH [p: ax|ax] {causation: pavilion ↔ pitched} --> \
> Night, black-clad, || negated morning, <!-- 2+2: NIGHT BLACK-CLAD || NEGATED MORNING [n: ax|ax] {opposition: Night ↔ morning} --> \
> oldest of all, || enthroned as mate; <!-- 2+2: OLDEST ALL || ENTHRONED MATE [vowel: aa|ax] {causation: oldest ↔ enthroned} --> \
> Orcus and Hades, || awe-named Demogorgon, <!-- 2+2: ORCUS HADES || AWE-NAMED DEMOGORGON [vowel: ax|ax] {causation: awe ↔ named} --> \
> Rumor ran, || rending names; <!-- 2+2: RUMOR RAN || RENDING NAMES [r: aa|ax] {violence: rending ↔ names} --> \
> Chance cheated || choosing from cause, <!-- 2+2: CHANCE CHEATED || CHOOSING CAUSE [ch: aa|ax] {privation: Chance ↔ choosing} --> \
> Tumult and Confusion || tangled each other, <!-- 2+2: TUMULT CONFUSION || TANGLED OTHER [t: ax|ax] {like-on-like: Tumult ↔ tangled} --> \
> Discord, thousand-mouthed, || divided all. <!-- 2+2: DISCORD THOUSAND-MOUTHED || DIVIDED ALL [d: ax|ax] {causation: Discord ↔ divided} --> \
> </div>

> Para 1 (ll.1-9): the griffin simile (theft, pursuit) opening into the fiend's chase—terrain catalog, limb catalog, verb catalog, each compressed onto arguing staves where the source's own antitheses permit.
> Para 2 (ll.10-17): the uproar as mutiny of the air; its assault on the ear; the fiend's resolve; the quest-question—the way to the coast where darkness wrecks on light.

> <!-- SCANSION KEY
> Audit comments only: stressed lifts in CAPS; stave letter and pattern in brackets using single-pipe notation (aa|ax), never the verse caesura mark; tension tags in braces drawn from {opposition | privation | causation | like-on-like | inversion | violence}; {none} marks an honest defect retained, with its reason.
> Pronunciation commitments: Arimaspians = ar-i-MAS-pi-ans (staves on m); Demogorgon = de-mo-GOR-gon (staves on g); Chaos = KAY-os (k); Hades = HAY-deez (h); Orcus = OR-kus (vowel); pavilion = pa-VIL-yun (stress on VIL, so the word staves on v, never p); onward = ON-ward (vowel).

> <!-- COMMENTARY
> FIDELITY MAP. Simile (ll.1-4): griffin, wilderness ("the wind"/"high fells" absorbing "winged course over hill"), marshy dale ("weltering fens"), Arimaspians, stealth-theft, guarded gold—all present; "by stealth had stolen" kept as the st-stave of l.4. Chase (ll.5-9): bog/steep = fen/scarp; strait/rough = "the strait and the stark" (rough -> stark for the stave); dense/rare = thick/thin; "pursues his way" = "thrusting his way" (l.7); head/hands/wings/feet = head/hands/heel/wing (feet -> heel, synecdoche for the stave); the five verbs swims/sinks/wades/creeps/flies kept entire and finite (l.9). "So eagerly" is absorbed into the verb-momentum of ll.5-9 rather than carried by an adverb. Uproar (ll.10-12): "universal uproar wild" = "an uproar at last, all the air in revolt" (universal -> all the air; wild -> in revolt, an amplification serving the inversion-tension); "stunning sounds and voices all confused" = "a static of voices stunned out of sense"; "borne through the hollow dark, assaults his ear with loudest vehemence" = "up the hollow it howls and hammers his ear" (dark dropped as ambient; vehemence folded into the force-verbs; assault -> hammers). Resolve and quest (ll.13-17): "thither he plies, undaunted" = l.13 ("din's heart" amplifies "thither" into the noise's center); "whatever power or spirit" = "whatever might or mind," the doublet split across the m-stave; "of the nethermost abyss might dwell in that noise" = ll.14-15 (abyss -> pit; dwell doubled into "nested," an appositive amplification serving the inversion); "which way the nearest coast of darkness lies, bordering on light" = ll.16-17 (nearest -> quickest course; the bordering rendered as shipwreck—an amplification that extends Milton's own coastal figure rather than importing a new one). Court (ll.18-27): "When lo" = "Look —"; throne of Chaos = "kept court enthroned"; "dark pavilion spread wide on the wasteful deep" = "night-spun pavilion on the naked deep" (spread-wide compressed; wasteful -> naked, privation for privation); "sable-vested" = "black-robed"; "consort of his reign" = "bride of his reign"; "eldest of things" = "eldest of all things made" (sharpened: the unmade elder of the made); Orcus and Hades in order, their apposition made explicit as "his echo, Hades" (amplification: the two are synonymous death-names); "the dreaded name of Demogorgon" = l.23, Milton's inversion (a name attends court) intensified by repetition; Rumor next, Chance ("reckless" amplifies Chance as the unreckoning power), Tumult and Confusion "all embroiled" = "tangled in one brawl"; "Discord with a thousand various mouths" expanded to ll.26-27, "various" unpacked as differing/untuned—the closing doubling is where most of the 1.3x budget is spent, on Milton's rhetorical peak.
> KENNING LEDGER (coinages only; Milton's phrases carried over—eg. "coast of dark," l.16—are excluded). gold-robbed (l.1): privation—the guardian named by his loss. hoard-thieves (l.3): causation—agents branded by what they took. strong-guarded (l.4): opposition—the strength the stealth undid. static of voices (l.11): privation—living speech reduced to dead signal. din's heart (l.13): inversion—formless noise granted a center. reefs of light (l.17): opposition—brightness made a wrecking-ground for the dark. night-spun (l.19): causation/craft—a tent woven from darkness, fabric from un-stuff. naked deep (l.19): privation—the waste as the unclothed void. his echo, Hades (l.22): like-on-like—the second death-name as reverberation of the first. reckless Chance (l.24): privation—the power that never reckons. (Plain descriptive epithets claiming no tension—"high fells," "weltering fens," l.2—are translation furniture, not kennings.)
> VERIFICATION. Both directions checked: every ledger phrase above appears verbatim at its cited line; every FORM example ("where night lies wrecked || on the reefs of light" l.17; "stole by stealth," "strong-guarded" l.4; "static," "stunned," "sense" l.11; "uproar," "air" l.10; "eldest," "all" l.21; "Orcus," "echo" l.22; "fells," "fens" l.2) appears verbatim in the poem.
> -->
>
> <div class="poem">
> As a griffin, gold-robbed, || gallops the wind, <!-- 2+2: GRIF-fin GOLD-robbed || GAL-lops WIND [g: aa|ax] {privation: griffin ↔ gold-robbed—the guardian named by his loss} --> \
> wings over high fells || and weltering fens, <!-- 2+2: WINGS FELLS || WEL-ter-ing FENS [w: ax|ax] {opposition: wings ↔ weltering—air-course over the mire that cannot hold him} (incidental f: fells/fens, non-structural) --> \
> hunting the Arimaspians, || the hoard-thieves <!-- 2+2: HUNT-ing ar-i-MAS-pi-ans || HOARD-THIEVES [h: ax|ax] {violence: hunting ↔ hoard-thieves—predator bound to prey} --> \
> who stole by stealth || the strong-guarded gold: <!-- 2+2: STOLE STEALTH || STRONG-guard-ed GOLD [st: aa|ax] {opposition: stealth ↔ strong-guarded—the silent against the fortified} --> \
> so the fiend flies || over fen and scarp, <!-- 2+2: FIEND FLIES || FEN SCARP [f: aa|ax] {opposition: flies ↔ fen—wings against sucking ground} --> \
> through the strait and the stark || he strives onward, <!-- 2+2: STRAIT STARK || STRIVES ON-ward [st: aa|ax] {opposition: strait ↔ strives—the narrows against the forcer} --> \
> through thick and through thin, || thrusting his way; <!-- 2+2: THICK THIN || THRUST-ing WAY [th: aa|ax] {opposition: thick ↔ thin—Milton's dense/rare set at war on one stave} --> \
> with head and hands, || with heel and wing, <!-- 2+2: HEAD HANDS || HEEL WING [h: aa|ax] {none—Milton's limb-catalog kept whole; retained for fidelity} --> \
> he swims, he sinks, || he wades, creeps, flies. <!-- 2+3: SWIMS SINKS || WADES CREEPS FLIES [s: aa|xxx] {opposition: swims ↔ sinks—buoyancy against drowning} guardrail: five lifts, stave confined to the a-verse—Milton's five-verb catalog kept entire (sw- = plain s- per cluster rule) --> \
>
> An uproar at last, || all the air in revolt, <!-- 2+2: UP-roar LAST || AIR re-VOLT [vowel: ax|ax] {inversion: uproar ↔ air-in-revolt—the medium made mutineer} ("all" scanned as dip) --> \
> a static of voices || stunned out of sense, <!-- 2+2: STAT-ic VOI-ces || STUNNED SENSE [st: ax|ax] {privation: stunned ↔ sense—speech battered past meaning} ("sense" = plain s-, unstaved per cluster rule) --> \
> up the hollow it howls || and hammers his ear. <!-- 2+2: HOL-low HOWLS || HAM-mers EAR [h: aa|ax] {violence: hammers ↔ ear—the sound strikes, the organ takes it; secondary causation: hollow ↔ howls, the void's shape begetting its voice} --> \
> Undaunted he drives || toward the din's heart, <!-- 2+2: DAUNT-ed DRIVES || DIN'S HEART [d: aa|ax] {opposition: undaunted ↔ din—fearlessness aimed into the fear-noise} --> \
> to meet whatever might || or mind there dwells, <!-- 2+2: MEET MIGHT || MIND DWELLS [m: aa|ax] {opposition: might ↔ mind—Milton's power-or-spirit doublet split across the stave} --> \
> nested in the noise || of the nethermost pit, <!-- 2+2: NEST-ed NOISE || NETH-er-most PIT [n: aa|ax] {inversion: nested ↔ noise—sound made a dwelling, the formless given architecture} --> \
> to ask the quickest course || to the coast of dark, <!-- 2+2: QUICK-est COURSE || COAST DARK [k: aa|ax] {none—wayfinding line retained for fidelity (Milton's "which way the nearest coast"); the argument lands in the next line} --> \
> where night lies wrecked || on the reefs of light. <!-- 2+2: NIGHT WRECKED || REEFS LIGHT [r: xa|ax] {violence: wrecked ↔ reefs—darkness shipwrecked on the border of light} --> \
>
> Look—there Chaos || kept court enthroned, <!-- 2+2: LOOK CHA-os || COURT en-THRONED [k: xa|ax] {opposition: Chaos ↔ court—unrule holding rule's ceremony} ("kept" scanned as dip) --> \
> his night-spun pavilion || on the naked deep, <!-- 2+2: NIGHT-spun pa-VIL-ion || NA-ked DEEP [n: ax|ax] {causation: night ↔ spun—a tent woven from darkness; secondary privation: the naked deep} --> \
> black-robed beside him || sat the bride of his reign, <!-- 2+2: BLACK-robed SIDE || BRIDE REIGN [b: ax|ax] {opposition: black ↔ bride—mourning-cloth worn as wedding-dress} ("sat" scanned as dip) --> \
> Night, the eldest || of all things made; <!-- 2+2: NIGHT ELD-est || ALL MADE [vowel: xa|ax] {opposition: eldest ↔ all-things-made—the unmade senior to creation} --> \
> Orcus stood by them || with his echo, Hades, <!-- 2+2: OR-cus STOOD || ECH-o HAY-deez [vowel: ax|ax] {like-on-like: Orcus ↔ echo—death's second name as reverberation of the first} --> \
> and the dreaded name, || the name Demogorgon; <!-- 2+2: DREAD-ed NAME || NAME dem-o-GOR-gon [n: xa|ax] {like-on-like: name ↔ name—the repetition is the entity; only a word attends court} --> \
> next ran Rumor || with reckless Chance, <!-- 2+2: RAN RU-mor || RECK-less CHANCE [r: aa|ax] {inversion: Rumor ↔ ran—talk given legs} ("next" as anacrusis) --> \
> Tumult and Confusion || tangled in one brawl, <!-- 2+2: TU-mult con-FU-sion || TAN-gled BRAWL [t: ax|ax] {like-on-like: Tumult ↔ tangled—disorder knotted into disorder; "all embroiled"} --> \
> and Discord, dinning || from differing mouths, <!-- 2+2: DIS-cord DIN-ning || DIF-fer-ing MOUTHS [d: aa|ax] {like-on-like: dinning ↔ differing—one din made of disagreements; the goddess of difference speaks only differences} --> \
> a thousand tongues, || none tuned to the next. <!-- 2+2: THOU-sand TONGUES || TUNED NEXT [t: xa|ax] {privation: tongues ↔ tuned—a chorus defined by failed unison} ("thousand" = th-, unstaved; "next" = n-, final lift) -->
> </div>
